# Recruitment & Hiring Tracker — Decision Log

Living record of every design/schema/process decision made on the real project (not the lecturer activity demo). Updated automatically as we go. If a decision changes later, the entry is edited in place with the new reasoning — nothing gets silently overwritten without a note.

Status key: **Done** = migrated/implemented and verified. **Pending** = decided but not yet built. **Open** = decision made but downstream implementation not started.

---

## Schema — Role & Status Enums

**Role enum** — `HR, INTERVIEWER, MANAGEMENT, HIRING_MANAGER, IT_ADMIN, LEADERSHIP_MANAGEMENT`. Unchanged all project. **Done.**

**ApplicationStatus enum** — `APPLIED, SHORTLISTED, REJECTED, IN_PROGRESS, HIRED`. `ON_HOLD` removed. Reasoning: once `CandidateApplication.hiringDecision` exists as the real final-outcome field, an `ON_HOLD` application status was redundant with `IN_PROGRESS` and added ambiguity about what "on hold" meant at the application level. **Done.**

**VacancyStatus enum** — `OPEN, CLOSED, ON_HOLD`. Kept as-is. You initially suggested removing `ON_HOLD` here too for consistency with `ApplicationStatus`; flagged the contradiction, you confirmed a vacancy being paused (e.g., budget freeze, role re-scoped) is a genuinely different, legitimate state from a candidate's status — so this one stays. **Done.**

**User.phoneNumber** — **correction, 2026-08-31: this entry was stale.** It originally said "not added... not needed," but the schema now has `User.phoneNumber String?` with a real migration (`20260831103000_add_user_phone_number`) already applied against the dev DB. Found while auditing the project for "is everything actually done" -- this line was never updated when the field was added (in a separate pass this log doesn't otherwise capture), so it flatly contradicted the current schema. Not a functionality problem, just a documentation gap; correcting the record here rather than leaving a misleading entry in place. **Done** (built, migrated).

---

## Schema — Hiring Decision & Feedback Redesign

**HiringDecision enum + fields** — new enum `HIRE, REJECT`; added to `CandidateApplication` as `hiringDecision` (nullable), plus `decidedByUserId` (FK → User, nullable) and `decidedAt` (nullable). Reasoning: the system had no actual field for "this candidate got hired/rejected" — that decision now lives here as the single source of truth. **Done.**

**Feedback.recommendation removed entirely** — along with the `Recommendation` enum (`HIRE, REJECT, NEXT_STAGE, HOLD`). Reasoning: once the real hire/reject decision lives on `CandidateApplication.hiringDecision`, having a second "recommendation" field on `Feedback` created two competing sources of truth for the same outcome. You explicitly chose full removal over trimming the enum. **Done.**

---

## Schema — Multi-Interviewer Panels

**InterviewPanelist join table added; `Interview.interviewerId` dropped.** Reasoning: the DB only supported a single interviewer per interview; real interviews can have a panel. New table: `id, interviewId (FK), userId (FK)`, unique on `(interviewId, userId)`. **Done.**

**Feedback unique constraint changed** from `@unique(interviewId)` to `@@unique([interviewId, interviewerId])`. Reasoning: knock-on effect of the panel change — with multiple panelists, each one needs to be able to submit their own feedback for the same interview, so the uniqueness has to be per (interview, interviewer) pair, not per interview alone. Caught before it caused a real bug. **Done.**

---

## Schema — Candidate Phone Number

**Candidate.phoneNumber added — nullable in the database (`String?`), required at the frontend form and backend validation layers.** Reasoning: your lecturer requires HR to both call and email candidates (call is manual — no telephony/auto-dial integration, the system just stores the number for HR to dial themselves). Tempting to make it `NOT NULL`, but rejected: you don't have a candidate-intake flow built yet, and if candidate records ever get created before a phone number is known (e.g., CV-upload-first flow), a hard DB constraint would block candidate creation outright. Nullable in the DB + required in the "Add Candidate" form + required in backend validation gets the same practical guarantee (HR can't normally skip it) without that failure mode. **Migration done and verified** (`20260814154416_add_candidate_phone_number`, run from the correct `backend` directory, DB in sync). **Still pending:** form/backend validation enforcement — not built yet, since the candidate creation flow doesn't exist yet.

---

## Auth Design

**Two login endpoints** — `POST /api/auth/login` (all roles except IT_ADMIN) and `POST /api/auth/admin-login` (IT_ADMIN only). Kept the existing `/api/auth/` prefix rather than switching to the bare `/login` + `/admin` from the original flowchart, since the prefix was already in place and working. **Done.**

**Identical generic failure message across all rejection reasons** — `"Invalid email or password"` — for unknown email, wrong password, inactive account, and wrong-door attempts (e.g., non-admin hitting `/admin-login`). Reasoning: prevents leaking which specific check failed (standard practice — don't tell an attacker whether the email exists). Chosen over the earlier-drafted `"Invalid credentials."` wording. **Done, verified** — all 4 rejection paths return byte-identical responses.

**isActive check added** — was missing from the original login code entirely; login now checks email → password → isActive → role, in that order, on both endpoints. **Done.**

---

## Scope Addition — Automated Emails

**Decision: build it, not abandon it.** Lecturer-approved addition: email interviewer(s) when HR schedules an interview; email candidate when an interview is scheduled and when the final hire/reject decision is made.

Reasoning it doesn't require undoing anything: it's additive logic layered onto endpoints you haven't built yet (interview creation, hiring decision finalization), not a change to auth or existing schema.

Reasoning on DB impact: mostly none required. `Candidate.email` and `User.email` already exist as the send-to addresses; `CandidateApplication.hiringDecision` already exists as the hire/reject trigger. Optional-but-recommended addition not yet decided: a notification/email log table (`recipientEmail, type, relatedApplicationId, sentAt, status`) for debugging and demo-ability — not required for the feature to function. **Open** — feature not started; email log table not decided.

**Email failure must not block the underlying action.** E.g., if the "interview scheduled" email fails to send, the interview still gets saved — the failure is caught and logged, not surfaced as a hard error to HR. **Decided**, not yet implemented (no email-sending code exists yet).

**Candidate email ≠ candidate login.** Sending candidates one-way notification emails does not conflict with the earlier decision that candidates have no system account — it only requires their stored email address, not a `User` row. Clarified to avoid confusion with that earlier scope boundary.

---

## Process / Tooling Notes (for recurring reference)

- **Prisma migration P3018 (duplicate FK)** — known Prisma migration-diff bug; if you hit "Duplicate foreign key constraint name" again, check the generated `migration.sql` for a redundant `ADD CONSTRAINT` on an FK that was never dropped in that migration, and delete just that block.
- **`prisma migrate reset`** — safe to use to rebuild the dev DB from scratch as long as there's no real data worth protecting (true for this project so far — only seeded test accounts exist).
- **`seed.ts` needs its own `import "dotenv/config"`** as the first line — Prisma's auto-seed subprocess does not inherit the parent CLI's environment variables, even though `server.ts` doesn't need this import itself.
- **Run `npx prisma generate` after every schema change**, before touching the seed or app code — a stale generated client throws misleading "Unknown argument" errors that look like a code bug but are actually just an out-of-date client.
- **Don't use `curl.exe` from PowerShell to test JSON POST endpoints** — PowerShell mangles embedded quotes when calling external `.exe`s. Use `Invoke-RestMethod ... -Body '...'` wrapped in `try/catch` (catch surfaces the real response body via `$_.ErrorDetails.Message`, which `Invoke-RestMethod` otherwise swallows on non-2xx responses).

## 16-Item Backend Build-Out (post schema/auth stage)

Ordered list agreed for everything remaining before wireframes are needed (wireframes only block frontend screens, not backend work): 1) frontend auth pipeline, 2) Vacancy endpoints, 3) Candidate endpoints, 4) CandidateApplication endpoints, 5) Interview + InterviewPanelist endpoints, 6) Feedback endpoints, 7) Feedback modification audit trail (new table), 8) Hiring decision + HM comparison endpoint, 9) IT Admin endpoints, 10) System-wide audit logs (new table), 11) Last-reviewed-CV tracking (new fields), 12) Bulk CV upload backend, 13) Email sending logic, 14) Automated reminder logic, 15) Reports data endpoints, 16) Role-based access applied across all of the above. Agreed approach: build and fully test each item before starting the next, rather than batching — proven necessary by how many separate real bugs item 1 alone surfaced (see below).

**Working folder connected directly** (`D:\Uni\Second Year\Second Sem\PPPM\Group Assignment\Node`) partway through item 1, after confirming with the user that AI-assisted code ("vibe coding") is an explicit, sanctioned part of this assignment — resolves the earlier open question about whether direct file access changes any academic-integrity exposure (it doesn't; the exposure, if any, was already present from copy-pasting AI-written code, which was already happening).

### Item 1 — Frontend auth pipeline: DONE, verified

Files created directly in `frontend/src`: `api/auth.ts`, `context/AuthContext.tsx` (localStorage-persisted), `routes/ProtectedRoute.tsx`, `pages/dashboards/RoleDashboard.tsx` (placeholder), `pages/Login.css`, `pages/Login.tsx`, `pages/AdminLogin.tsx`; replaced `App.tsx` and `main.tsx`. All 6 roles verified logging in and landing on the correct dashboard; disabled account correctly rejected.

Real bugs hit and fixed along the way (kept here since they're likely to recur and are now known fixes):
- A stray empty file `src/api/auth` (no extension) shadowed the real `auth.ts` in module resolution — caused a blank page. Fix: delete the extensionless file.
- `react-router-dom` was installed into `backend` by mistake instead of `frontend` — fix: uninstall from the wrong folder, install in the right one.
- A `frontend` install of `react-router-dom` was corrupted (missing `package.json`/`dist`) — fix: uninstall + reinstall.
- Vite's dependency cache went stale after the reinstall — fix: `npm run dev -- --force` once.
- Multiple zombie `npm run dev` processes accumulated from repeated restarts (ports 5173/5174/5175 all in use at once) — fix: End Task on all `Node.js JavaScript Runtime` processes in Task Manager, restart clean.
- **Both servers must stay running simultaneously in two separate terminal panes at all times** — closing/reusing one terminal to run the other server is the single most common failure mode seen this session (`ECONNREFUSED` / "ends up looking like a JSON error but isn't"). Always check both panes show live, current output before testing.
- Database connection pool timeouts (`pool timeout ... active=0 idle=0`) despite MySQL running fine and Workbench connecting instantly — root cause was the `root` user's auth plugin being `caching_sha2_password`, which the `mariadb` npm driver doesn't negotiate cleanly (hangs instead of erroring). Fixed permanently with `ALTER USER 'root'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED WITH mysql_native_password BY '<password>'; FLUSH PRIVILEGES;` — this is a one-time database-side fix, already applied, should not recur.

### Item 2 — Vacancy endpoints: DONE, verified

Files: `backend/src/controllers/vacancy.controller.ts`, `backend/src/routes/vacancy.routes.ts`, wired into `app.ts` at `/api/vacancies`.

Endpoints: `POST /api/vacancies` (create), `GET /api/vacancies` (list, optional `?status=` filter), `GET /api/vacancies/:id` (detail, includes stages), `PATCH /api/vacancies/:id` (edit any field including `status`), `POST /:id/stages`, `PATCH /:id/stages/:stageId`, `DELETE /:id/stages/:stageId`.

**Permission rules (confirmed, tested, locked in):**
- HR only: create vacancies, edit vacancies, create/edit/delete interview stages.
- Any logged-in role: can view vacancies and their stages (read-only).
- Closing a vacancy is done via `PATCH .../:id` with `{"status":"CLOSED"}`, not a delete — vacancies are never hard-deleted (matches the backlog requirement to retain records after closure).
- Deleting a stage that already has interviews scheduled against it is blocked (returns 409) rather than silently orphaning data.

All 8 test cases passed against the real database: create, list, get-one, add-stage, close, non-HR blocked from creating (403 with the standard permission message), non-HR confirmed can still view, edit-stage, delete-stage.

### Item 3 — Candidate endpoints: DONE, verified

Files: `backend/src/controllers/candidate.controller.ts`, `backend/src/routes/candidate.routes.ts`, wired into `app.ts` at `/api/candidates`.

Endpoints: `POST /api/candidates` (create), `GET /api/candidates` (list, optional `?search=` matches name/email), `GET /api/candidates/:id` (detail, includes full application history across vacancies), `PATCH /api/candidates/:id` (edit).

**Rules:** same pattern as Vacancy — HR only for create/edit, any logged-in role can view. No delete endpoint at all, by design — candidates are never removable, satisfying the backlog's "retain candidate records after vacancy closure" requirement structurally rather than with special-case logic.

**Deliberately scoped smaller than the backlog wording:** the backlog's "search and filter candidates by vacancy, stage, score, and status" is bigger than this item — vacancy/stage live on `CandidateApplication` (item 4), score lives on `Feedback` (item 6). This item only does name/email search. Full cross-table filtering gets built when those items are done.

Real bug hit: same stale-generated-Prisma-Client issue as before (`Unknown argument phoneNumber`), even though the field had been migrated a while ago — the client just hadn't been regenerated recently. Fixed with `npx prisma generate` + a manual backend restart (`tsx watch` doesn't pick up changes to the `generated/` folder on its own). **Recurring gotcha to remember going forward: after any `npx prisma generate`, the backend terminal needs a manual restart, not just a save.**

All 5 test cases passed: create, list, get-one (empty application history correctly shown), search by name, non-HR blocked from creating.

### Item 4 — CandidateApplication endpoints: DONE, verified

Files: `backend/src/controllers/application.controller.ts`, `backend/src/routes/application.routes.ts` (mounted at `/api/applications`), plus two routes added onto the existing `vacancyRouter` (`/api/vacancies/:id/applications`) since applying/listing-by-vacancy is naturally vacancy-scoped.

Endpoints: `POST /api/vacancies/:id/applications` (apply a candidate to a vacancy, HR only), `GET /api/vacancies/:id/applications` (list applications for a vacancy, any role), `GET /api/applications/:id` (single application with candidate + vacancy nested), `PATCH /api/applications/:id/status` (HR or INTERVIEWER only — matches backlog: HR shortlists/rejects, interviewers progress candidates through stages).

**Important scoping decision:** this only touches the `status` field (APPLIED/SHORTLISTED/REJECTED/IN_PROGRESS/HIRED). The separate `hiringDecision` field (final HIRE/REJECT) is untouched — that's item 8's job specifically. Do not conflate the two when building later items — `status` is pipeline progression, `hiringDecision` is the final formal outcome.

A candidate can only apply once per vacancy (`@@unique([candidateId, vacancyId])` in schema) — verified the duplicate attempt fails cleanly with a 409, not a crash.

All 6 test cases passed: apply, list-by-vacancy (candidate nested), get-one (candidate + vacancy both nested), status update to SHORTLISTED, duplicate-application blocked, Management correctly blocked from changing status (only HR/INTERVIEWER allowed).

### Standing rule — when to automate testing (2026-08-21)

User asked me to own this decision going forward without being asked each time. Rule:

- Backend endpoints: automate immediately after building each one (PowerShell `Invoke-RestMethod` scripts, same as every item so far) — cheap to rerun, catches real bugs fast, behavior is stable once an endpoint is written.
- Frontend UI (Selenium): NOT while a screen is still being built/iterated on. Do a manual click-through first to confirm it works, and only write Selenium automation once that screen's layout/selectors have settled — writing it too early means rewriting selectors every time the UI changes.
- Manual QA documentation (US-XX test case tables): written after a feature (backend + frontend for that story) is fully working and stable, not mid-build, since the point is to record a finished feature's full scenario coverage for grading.

I'll apply this automatically after finishing each build item and tell the user which category it falls into, rather than asking each time.

### Item #20 — Bulk CV upload: DONE, verified

User chose real CSV file upload over pasting JSON. Added `multer` (memory storage) + `csv-parse`. `POST /api/candidates/bulk` (HR only, multipart field name `file`) parses a CSV and creates candidates row-by-row, reporting per-row success/failure rather than all-or-nothing (duplicate email, missing fields, etc. each reported individually with the row number).

Two real bugs hit and fixed: (1) Windows PowerShell's `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` writes a BOM that corrupted the first CSV header — fixed with `bom: true` in the parse options. (2) My own bug: CSV headers get lowercased for case-insensitivity (`cvUrl` -> `cvurl`), but the row-processing code was still reading `row.cvUrl`/`row.phoneNumber` (camelCase, always undefined) — fixed by reading the lowercased keys. Also noted for future reference: this Windows PowerShell version doesn't support `Invoke-RestMethod -Form` (needs PS 6+); use `curl.exe` (not the `curl` alias) for multipart file uploads instead.

- `GET /api/vacancies/:id/report` — single-vacancy rollup: status counts, total interviews, average feedback score across all interviews for that vacancy.

Tested all 4 against real data (vacancy 1: CLOSED, 1 application, HIRED, 1 interview with feedback score 7; vacancies 2-5: OPEN/junk test vacancies, 0 applications): dashboard correctly excluded vacancy 1 and listed 2-5 with zero counts; stage-monitoring showed stage "Technical Interview" (id 2) with `candidateCount: 1`, `noStageAssigned: 0`; KPIs returned `applicationsReceived: 1, hires: 1`, rest 0/1 as expected; per-vacancy report returned matching stats plus `averageFeedbackScore: 7`. No bugs found — first endpoint this session to pass all tests on the first try.

### Item #24 — Full regression pass: DONE, verified

Built `regression-test.ps1` at the project root (not inside `backend`/`frontend` — it's a whole-API test, and separate from the Python/Selenium `automated-tests/` folder which covers UI only). Covers 49 checks across auth (all 6 roles + disabled account + wrong password + IT Admin two-door + forgot/reset password), vacancies, candidates (incl. CV-review-tracking overwrite behavior), applications (incl. duplicate-blocked), interviews/panelists, feedback + audit trail (incl. duplicate-feedback-blocked, edit-without-reason-blocked), stage progression (role-gated), hiring decision (role-gated), and all 4 reports endpoints. Creates its own timestamped vacancy/candidate each run so it never collides with prior data — safe to re-run anytime as a smoke test after future changes.

Real bug hit and fixed: the script itself, not the backend. Used an em-dash (`—`) in two `Write-Host` strings; the file was saved without a UTF-8 BOM, so Windows PowerShell 5.1 read it under the system codepage, misinterpreted the multi-byte em-dash, and lost a string terminator (`ParseException: TerminatorExpectedAtEndOfString`). Fixed by replacing both em-dashes with plain hyphens. **New standing rule: no non-ASCII characters (em-dashes, curly quotes, etc.) in any `.ps1` file** — Windows PowerShell 5.1 scripts should be treated as ASCII-only unless explicitly saved with a BOM.

First full run: **49/49 passed, zero real backend bugs found.** This is the strongest confirmation yet that items #1 through #23 are genuinely stable, not just individually-tested-and-forgotten.

### Items #31/#32 — Automated emails: DONE, verified

Implements the scope addition logged earlier ("Scope Addition — Automated Emails"). Both wired into existing endpoints, no new endpoints/schema needed, using the existing dev-only `sendEmail()` from `mailer.ts`.

**#31 (`POST /api/applications/:id/interviews`, in `interview.controller.ts`):** after the interview + panelists are created, emails go to (a) every panelist ("you've been assigned to interview X for Y role, stage Z, scheduled for...") and (b) the candidate ("your interview for Y at Altrium has been scheduled..."). Include clause extended to pull `stage`, `application.candidate`, `application.vacancy` for email content.

**#32 (`PATCH /api/applications/:id/decision`, in `application.controller.ts`):** after the hire/reject decision is saved, one email goes to the candidate — a congratulations/offer message on HIRE, a polite rejection message on REJECT.

**Both wrapped in try/catch that only logs on failure** — matches the standing rule that email failure must never block the underlying action (interview still gets scheduled / decision still gets recorded even if the "send" throws).

Tested against real data (regression-test.ps1's application id 3): scheduled a second interview on it, confirmed both DEV EMAIL blocks (interviewer + candidate) printed with correct recipient/subject/body; then flipped the same application's decision to REJECT and confirmed the reject-path email printed correctly. No bugs found.

### SECURITY BUG — passwordHash/resetToken fields leaking in API responses: FOUND AND FIXED

Found while testing item #33 below. `CandidateApplication.hiringManager` (freshly added) returned the full `User` row in the JSON response, including `passwordHash` (the bcrypt hash) and `resetTokenHash`. Checked the rest of the codebase: this wasn't new to that one endpoint — every existing controller that nests a `User` via `include` (`decidedBy` in hiring decision/comparison, `interviewer` in feedback, `user` in panelists, `lastCvReviewedBy` on candidates, `editedBy` in the feedback audit log) had the same problem the whole time. Nobody had actually looked at the full raw JSON of those nested objects before — prior tests checked specific fields (`.name`, `.role`) and never printed the whole nested user object, so this went unnoticed since item #5.

**Fix: one global `omit` on the Prisma client** (`backend/src/prisma.ts`) rather than patching `select` into every individual `include: { user: true }` site across 7 controllers — a per-site fix relies on every future include remembering to exclude these fields, which is exactly how this happened in the first place.

```ts
export const prisma = new PrismaClient({
  adapter,
  omit: { user: { passwordHash: true, resetTokenHash: true, resetTokenExpiresAt: true } },
});
```

**This broke 3 read sites that legitimately need those fields**, all in `auth.controller.ts`: `login` and `adminLogin` need `user.passwordHash` for `bcrypt.compare`; `resetPassword` needs `user.resetTokenExpiresAt` to check token expiry. Fixed by overriding the omit per-query at exactly those 3 call sites (`omit: { passwordHash: false }` / `omit: { resetTokenExpiresAt: false }`) — Prisma allows a per-query omit override of the client-level default.

Verified: (1) HR login still works after the change, (2) re-fetched the same `hiringManager` object and confirmed `passwordHash`/`resetTokenHash` are now absent while `id/name/email/role/department/isActive/createdAt` still come through, (3) ran a full real forgot-password -> reset-password -> login-with-new-password round trip end to end to confirm the `resetTokenExpiresAt` override didn't silently break token expiry checking, (4) reset HR's password back to `password123` afterward so it doesn't diverge from every other test script that assumes the seeded password.

**Standing rule going forward:** when adding any new `include: { someUser: true }` in a controller, no action needed — the global omit already applies. Only touch `omit` overrides if a new code path needs to read `passwordHash`/`resetTokenHash`/`resetTokenExpiresAt` directly (rare, should stay confined to `auth.controller.ts`).

### Item #33 — Assign Hiring Manager to a candidate application: DONE, verified

Real gap confirmed and fixed (SCRUM2-9): there was no way to designate which specific Hiring Manager owns a given application — `recordHiringDecision` was open to any HIRING_MANAGER-role user with no assignment concept at all, unlike interviewers who get explicitly assigned via `InterviewPanelist`.

Schema: added `CandidateApplication.hiringManagerId` (nullable FK) + `hiringManager` relation, plus the inverse `User.assignedHmOn`. Migration `add_application_hiring_manager`.

Endpoints: `PATCH /api/applications/:id/assign-hm` (HR only) — validates the target user exists and has role `HIRING_MANAGER`, else 400. `GET /api/applications/assigned-to-me` (HIRING_MANAGER only) — lists applications assigned to the calling HM; registered *before* `GET /:id` in the router since Express would otherwise match the literal path `assigned-to-me` as an `:id` value.

**Deliberately left open, not decided unilaterally:** `recordHiringDecision` (item #16) still allows *any* HIRING_MANAGER to record a decision, not just the one assigned via this new field. Tightening that to "only the assigned HM may decide" is a real product question (e.g. does it need to support backup/delegate HMs covering for someone) that the backlog story doesn't resolve on its own — flagging for you to decide rather than silently changing already-tested permission behavior.

Also fixed as a side effect: `GET /api/applications/:id` previously only included `candidate`/`vacancy`, silently omitting `currentStage`, `decidedBy`, and (now) `hiringManager` even though all three exist on the model — a caller had no way to see the full picture of an application in one call. Extended the include.

Tested against real data (application id 3): assign succeeds and returns nested `hiringManager`; non-HR blocked (403); assigning a user whose role isn't HIRING_MANAGER blocked (400); assigned HM sees it via `/assigned-to-me`; `GET /:id` now correctly surfaces `hiringManager`/`decidedBy`/`currentStage` by name. No further bugs found beyond the security issue above (which was caught here, then fixed and verified globally).

### Item — Reports: PDF export: DONE, verified

`GET /api/vacancies/:id/report/pdf` (any authenticated role, same access as the JSON version). Uses `pdfkit` (no native binaries, safe dependency for this stack) to stream a real PDF — same underlying data/query as item #23's `getVacancyReport`, rendered as a document instead of JSON.

Went through two passes: first pass was a plain unstyled data dump (functionally correct but visually bare), which the user correctly called out as looking unfinished. Second pass applied real Altrium branding: black header band, "ALTRIUM" wordmark, a simplified hexagon mark (drawn as vector shapes via `doc.polygon()` — flat-top hexagon, white arrow, gold dot, since there's no exported logo image asset to embed, only the React/SVG component), gold (#f5a623) accent bars on section headings, and a real bordered/shaded table for the status-count breakdown instead of a plain indented list.

This completes all 5 pieces the Sprint 1 WBS lists under "Reports" (dashboard, stage monitoring, KPIs, per-vacancy report, PDF export) — Reports is now fully done.

Verified visually: downloaded and opened the generated PDF, confirmed header band/hexagon/branding render correctly and all data (stages, status counts, interview count, average feedback score) matches the JSON version already tested in item #23.

## Full Backlog Audit + Pipeline Redesign (2026-08-21)

At the user's request, did a full audit of the actual detailed spec docs (`Acceptance criteria (1).docx`, ~50 stories, goes well beyond the plain backlog rows) plus the real Sprint 1/Sprint 2 backlog docs and several activity diagrams, rather than continuing to work from memory/conversational context. Found several real mismatches between what was built and what the spec actually says, most significantly: #31/#32 (emails) are Sprint 2 stories, not Sprint 1 (built ahead of schedule, not wrong, just early — left as-is per user decision); the Hiring Manager's "recommend progression" (US-19) has its own activity diagram showing it's a separate advisory action, not the same as the Interviewer's actual stage update, which is how item #30 had originally been built (wrong); US-05 (HR configures custom stages per vacancy) directly contradicts US-31 (fixed universal 7-stage list) — resolved by the user in favor of the universal list; "assign interviewers to a vacancy" (US-10) turned out to describe a standing pool assignment with availability/conflict checking, not the ad-hoc per-interview panelist assignment that existed.

User's resolution, in order, with one item (#5) corrected from the literal spec: universal 7-stage pipeline; keep `hiringManagerId` at the application level (not vacancy level — confirmed via the real "assign interviewers and hiring managers to shortlisted candidates" wording); separate HM recommendation from actual stage update; add stage-entry/exit history; make vacancy reports available for both open and closed vacancies (overriding US-37's literal "closed only" wording) with stage-progression/time-in-stage data added; vacancy-level interviewer pool + scheduling conflict check; duplicate-vacancy validation; KPI additions (open-vacancy count, average time to hire); strict no-skip/no-backwards stage movement; leave the Sprint-2-early email features alone.

**Pre-migration discipline (both suggested by the user, should have been my own first move):** ran a full dependency inventory (grep every reference to `VacancyStage`, `status`, `currentStageId`, `Interview.stageId` across the codebase) before touching schema, and committed all uncommitted work first — turned out 37 files/items across items #6-37 had never been committed since the last real commit (item 5), including 5 migrations, the reports/feedback controllers, the mailer, the regression script, and the security fix. Committed as `acb89a7` before starting the migration.

### Schema redesign: `VacancyStage` removed, `RecruitmentStage` enum introduced

New enum `RecruitmentStage`: `APPLIED, SHORTLISTED, INTERVIEW_1, INTERVIEW_2, FINAL_INTERVIEW, HIRED, REJECTED` (rank-ordered for the no-skip rule; `REJECTED` is the one exception, reachable from any rank). `CandidateApplication.status` + `currentStageId` merged into a single authoritative `stage` field. `Interview.stageId` (FK to the now-removed `VacancyStage`) became `Interview.stage` (same enum, but explicitly descriptive-only metadata — never the source of truth for the candidate's actual progression, which stays on `CandidateApplication.stage`). `Vacancy` gained `@@unique([title, department])` for duplicate rejection.

Three new tables: `ApplicationStageHistory` (entry/exit timestamps per stage transition, written by a shared helper in `backend/src/utils/stageTransition.ts` so every write site — application creation, HR shortlist/reject, Interviewer progression, HM hire/reject decision — logs consistently instead of four separate hand-rolled implementations), `StageRecommendation` (HM's advisory input, `applicationId/hiringManagerId/recommendation/comments`, never touches `stage`), `VacancyInterviewer` (HR's standing pool assignment per vacancy, `@@unique([vacancyId, userId])`).

Migration required a full `prisma migrate reset` rather than in-place `migrate dev` (too structurally invasive — dropping a table, changing a column's type from FK to enum). Only seed/test data existed, consistent with the established rule that reset is safe here.

### Controller changes (full list, so future-me doesn't have to re-derive this)

- `application.controller.ts`: `updateApplicationStatus` narrowed to HR-only-meaningful targets `SHORTLISTED`/`REJECTED`, only valid from `APPLIED`. `updateApplicationStage` is now Interviewer-only (HM removed), accepts only `INTERVIEW_1/2/FINAL_INTERVIEW`, enforces exact rank+1 via `STAGE_RANK`. New `submitStageRecommendation`/`listRecommendationsForApplication` (HM submits, anyone can view via `getApplication`). `recordHiringDecision` now routes through `transitionApplicationStage` with `hiringDecision/decidedByUserId/decidedAt` passed as extra atomic update data. `compareApplicationsForVacancy` filter updated to the new stage values.
- `candidate.controller.ts`: `listCandidates` collapsed the old separate `status`/`stageId` query params into one `stage` param (RecruitmentStage value), since they're now the same underlying field.
- `interview.controller.ts`: `scheduleInterview` now validates panelists are in the vacancy's `VacancyInterviewer` pool first (400 if not), then checks for an exact-timestamp conflict against any existing interview for those panelists (409 if found), before creating. Body takes `stage` instead of `stageId`.
- `vacancy.controller.ts`: `createVacancy`/`updateVacancy` catch `P2002` for the new duplicate constraint. Removed `addStage`/`updateStage`/`deleteStage` entirely. New `assignInterviewerToVacancy`/`removeInterviewerFromVacancy`/`listVacancyInterviewers` (role-gated to INTERVIEWER/MANAGEMENT/HIRING_MANAGER as assignable targets).
- `reports.controller.ts`: `getStageMonitoring` rewritten around `groupBy` on the fixed `RecruitmentStage` set (no more per-vacancy stage rows to join against). `getKpis` gained `openVacancies` and `averageTimeToHireDays` (computed from `appliedAt`→`decidedAt` on HIRED applications). Both `getVacancyReport` and `getVacancyReportPdf` gained `stageTimings` (average hours per stage, computed from `ApplicationStageHistory`, excluding still-open entries) via a new shared `computeStageTimings` helper and `buildVacancyReportData` function (deduplicates what was previously two near-identical copies of the same query/aggregation logic between the JSON and PDF endpoints).

### Regression: rewritten from scratch (v2), 48/48 passed

The old `regression-test.ps1` was built entirely around the removed fields and would have failed wholesale, so it was rewritten around the new model rather than patched. Real bug hit during this (mine, not the backend's): after reseeding via `npx prisma db seed`, every single check failed identically including basic login — turned out the backend process itself wasn't running (the user had to `Ctrl+C` the running `npm run dev` to free the terminal for the seed command, and never restarted it afterward). Diagnosed by having the user run a single raw `Invoke-RestMethod` login call outside the script, which surfaced "Unable to connect to the remote server" instead of the script's generic swallowed-exception failure — a reminder that the regression script's blanket try/catch hides the real error class (connection failure vs. actual 4xx) unless you drop back to a raw call. Once the backend was actually running, full pass: universal-stage no-skip/no-backwards enforcement, HM recommendation confirmed non-mutating, interviewer-pool + conflict-check validation, duplicate-vacancy rejection, stage-history entry/exit logging (6 entries for a full Applied→Hired lifecycle), and both new KPI fields all verified against real data.

User is reconsidering the earlier fixed-7-stage decision (see "US-05 (HR configures custom stages per vacancy) directly contradicts US-31" above) — raised the fair point that a single fixed pipeline doesn't fit every job role. Discussed three options: (1) keep the current fixed 7-stage model as-is, (2) hybrid — fixed Applied/Shortlisted/Hired/Rejected categories for reporting, with HR-configurable interview rounds per vacancy inside the "Interviewing" bucket (recommended, matches how real ATS tools like Greenhouse/Lever do it), (3) fully freeform per-vacancy stages per the literal US-05 wording, biggest rebuild since it breaks the current cross-vacancy dashboard aggregation (US-30-33, US-49). ## Stage-advancement authority reversed: Interviewer → HR (US-20)

**Decision:** `updateApplicationStage` (advancing a candidate through INTERVIEW_1 → INTERVIEW_2 → FINAL_INTERVIEW) moves from Interviewer-only to HR-only. **Done** — `application.routes.ts` role gate changed, `application.controller.ts` comment updated to explain why.

**Reasoning:** User traced through the actual workflow and found two real problems with the Interviewer-owns-it model: (1) US-19's Hiring Manager recommendation is documented as "visible to HR officers," not to the Interviewer — so the Interviewer wasn't guaranteed to even see the HM's input before independently advancing the candidate; the recommendation had no action tied to it anywhere. (2) `updateApplicationStage` had no panelist check (unlike `submitFeedback`, which does check `isPanelist`) — any user holding the Interviewer role could advance any candidate, not just ones they actually interviewed. Moving execution to HR gives every input an owner: Interviewer submits feedback, Hiring Manager recommends, HR reviews both and executes the move. Also closes the panel-attribution gap as a side effect, since Interviewers no longer hold that authority at all.

**This reverses US-20 as written** in the Acceptance Criteria, WBS, and Product Backlog (all currently say Interviewer). Those documents have not been edited yet — flagging so it isn't forgotten. **Downstream, not yet done:** the Manual Test Cases doc (US-20 rows) still say "Log in as Interviewer... update the candidate's stage" and need updating to "Log in as HR." No frontend screen exists yet for this action, so no frontend rework needed. `regression-test.ps1` likely still asserts Interviewer-only and will need updating before its next run.

## Correction: HR-authority version above was itself superseded within the same session

Immediately after the HR-authority change above, user pushed further: HR shouldn't be in this loop at all — the Hiring Manager's recommendation should be **binding**, not advisory. Clarified exactly what "binding" meant (three options: HR blocked from contradicting it / HM's recommendation IS the decision / HR needs one to exist but can override) — user chose **HM's recommendation IS the decision**.

**Implemented:** `submitStageRecommendation` (application.controller.ts) now directly mutates `CandidateApplication.stage`. `ADVANCE` moves the application to the next interview stage (reusing the same rank-based no-skip logic); if there's no next interview stage (already at Final Interview), it's rejected with an error directing to the hiring-decision endpoint instead. `DO_NOT_PROGRESS` transitions the application straight to `REJECTED` — read as "the recommendation is the decision, so a non-advance ends the candidate's run on this vacancy," same as any other rejection. The standalone `PATCH /:id/stage` route and its `requireRole(Role.HR)` gate were removed entirely from `application.routes.ts` — neither HR nor Interviewer has a direct stage-mutation action anymore. `updateApplicationStage` is left in the controller file as dead code/reference, not imported or routed.

**Net result, final state:** Interviewer submits feedback only (no stage authority, ever). Hiring Manager's recommendation is the sole trigger for interview-stage progression and functions as an immediate accept/reject at each stage. HR retains only the initial Applied→Shortlisted/Rejected call (`updateApplicationStatus`) and scheduling/admin duties — no role in the interview-progression pipeline. Hiring Manager separately still makes the final Hired call once a candidate reaches Final Interview (`recordHiringDecision`, unchanged, still binding, still checks `stage === FINAL_INTERVIEW`).

**This is now a second reversal of US-20/US-19 within the same session** (Interviewer → HR → Hiring-Manager-binding). WBS/Backlog/Acceptance Criteria docs still describe the original Interviewer-does-everything version and haven't been touched. **Still outstanding:** AC/WBS/Backlog text for US-19 and US-20, the Manual Test Cases doc's US-19/US-20 rows (need full rewrite, not just a role-name swap — the actual mechanics changed), and `regression-test.ps1`. Type-checked clean — no new TS errors introduced (the one pre-existing `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` warning on the `StageRecommendation` create call predates this session's changes).

## Stage catalog resolved: per-vacancy configurable interview rounds (US-05, finally decided)

**Decision:** the long-deferred US-05 vs US-31 conflict is resolved. Not a single universal fixed pipeline, and not fully freeform per-vacancy stages either — a middle model.

**Structure stays fixed, labels don't.** Applied, Shortlisted, Hired, Rejected remain fixed anchors (tied to existing hardcoded actions: CV screening, final hiring decision). Between Shortlisted and Hired/Rejected, HR defines however many interview rounds a given vacancy needs (1, 2, 3+), and can attach any free-text label to each round describing how it's actually conducted for that role ("Technical Screen," "Portfolio Review," "Panel Interview," whatever fits). Dashboards/reports (US-30-33, US-49) aggregate on numeric rank ("round 2"), never on the label, so cross-vacancy reporting stays intact no matter what each round is called or how differently jobs are actually interviewed.

**Editing rules once a vacancy exists:**
- Before any interviews have started (no candidate past Shortlisted): HR can add, remove, reorder, or rename interview rounds freely.
- Once interviews have started: three-tier lock —
  - **Completed** stages (anyone has passed through): locked, no change or reorder.
  - **Current** stage (anyone is sitting there right now): locked, no change or reorder.
  - **Future/unreached** stages (nobody has reached yet): still fully renameable and reorderable.
- New stages can only be **appended** after the furthest-progressed active (non-Hired/non-Rejected) candidate's position on that vacancy — never inserted mid-sequence. This falls out naturally from the current-stage lock: since every candidate's current stage is locked, and candidates can be at different points simultaneously, the union of all "completed + current" stages across every candidate defines the locked boundary; only what's beyond all of that is open.
- If a new stage is appended, every candidate who hasn't finished the process yet must go through it — nobody who's still active gets grandfathered around a newly added stage, but nobody already Hired/Rejected is retroactively affected either.

**Not yet implemented** — schema, migration, and controller logic for configurable per-vacancy interview rounds don't exist yet (current schema still has the old fixed `RecruitmentStage` enum with `INTERVIEW_1`/`INTERVIEW_2`/`FINAL_INTERVIEW` as fixed values). This is a real, sizeable build: vacancy-level stage definitions table, rank-based progression logic reworked to read from per-vacancy stage lists instead of a fixed enum, the three-tier lock validation, and updating every place that currently assumes the fixed 7-stage enum (reports, KPIs, `submitStageRecommendation`, `recordHiringDecision`'s Final-Interview check, the frontend stage board mockup). Acceptance Criteria (US-05), WBS, and Product Backlog all still describe the old fixed model and need rewriting to match.

---

## Audit log architecture resolved (US-21/US-43)

**Problem:** two overlapping AC stories — US-21 ("recruitment activity audit logs," IT Admin) and US-43 ("system-wide audit logging," IT Admin) — plus two existing purpose-built history tables (`ApplicationStageHistory`, `FeedbackAuditLog`) that already satisfy part of both stories without either doc acknowledging it.

**Decision — one generic `AuditLog` table for everything not already covered:**
- Columns: `userId`, `action`, `entityType`, `entityId`, `timestamp`, plus a metadata/details field (JSON) for action-specific extras (e.g. notification channel/recipient, CV filename).
- **Do not duplicate** stage transitions (Shortlisted, interview-stage advance/reject, Hired, Rejected) — these stay exclusively in `ApplicationStageHistory`. The Audit Log screen reads/merges from there rather than writing a second copy.
- **Do not duplicate** feedback edits — stay exclusively in `FeedbackAuditLog` (already captures previous/new score, previous/new comments, editor, timestamp).
- **New logging needed** (none of these write anywhere today): interview scheduled, vacancy created, vacancy edited, vacancy closed, CV uploaded, feedback submitted (initial creation — the edit path is already covered, the create path isn't), notification sent, login, account created, account deactivated, role changed.
- "Permission changes" (from US-43's wording) is not a distinct event — checked the schema, there's no separate permissions model, only the fixed `Role` enum gated via `requireRole()`. A user's role *is* their full permission set, so this collapses into "role changed," not two event types.

**Known open problems, not yet resolved — flag before building:**
1. **Merge-query cost.** A single IT Admin audit screen that's filterable by date range (per US-21/US-43's own AC) means querying `AuditLog` + `ApplicationStageHistory` + `FeedbackAuditLog` and merging/sorting/paginating across three differently-shaped tables — not a simple single-table read. Needs its own query design before the endpoint is built.
2. **Access control across mixed event types.** US-43 requires security logs (login, account create/deactivate, role change) be "accessible only to authorized IT staff." If those rows live in the same table as routine recruitment events (vacancy edited, feedback submitted), the audit endpoint needs row-level filtering by action/category so non-IT-Admin viewers of recruitment activity can't also see security events — not solved by table-level access control alone.
3. **JSON metadata trades away queryability.** Structured filters like "all vacancy edits in department X" require parsing JSON at query time (or generated/indexed columns later) rather than a plain WHERE clause. Accepted as an MVP tradeoff, not free.
4. **Write-site discipline.** No shared helper/middleware decided yet for who calls the audit-log write — risk of future features forgetting to log, the same failure mode `transitionApplicationStage()` was built to avoid for stage changes. Needs a single choke point, not scattered manual calls across every controller.
5. **Scope/timing.** This adds write-hooks to nearly every controller (auth, vacancy, feedback, candidate/CV upload, notifications) plus a new IT Admin screen — a real cross-cutting feature, not a small addition. Not yet scheduled against the sprint plan.

**Not yet implemented** — no `AuditLog` table, migration, or write calls exist yet for any of the 8 new event types (interview scheduled, vacancy created/edited/closed, CV uploaded, feedback submitted, notification sent, login, account created/deactivated, role changed).

---

## Audit log — 5 of 8 events built, 3 blocked on IT Admin

**Decision:** don't build IT Admin account management just to complete the audit log. Build logging for events that already have a real code path; mark the other 3 as explicitly blocked.

**Built (code written, migration not yet run):**
- Schema: `AuditLog` model added (`userId, action, entityType, entityId, metadata Json?, createdAt`, indexed on `[entityType, entityId]` and `createdAt`). Inverse relation `User.auditLogEntries`.
- `backend/src/utils/auditLog.ts` — `writeAuditLog()`, the single choke point for every write to this table, same pattern as `transitionApplicationStage()`. Directly resolves the "write-site discipline" open question from the earlier entry.
- `vacancy.controller.ts`: `createVacancy` → `VACANCY_CREATED`. `updateVacancy` → fetches the vacancy's status *before* the update so it can tell `VACANCY_CLOSED` (status transitions to `CLOSED`) apart from `VACANCY_EDITED` (anything else) — both go through the same function, so this diff was necessary, not optional.
- `candidate.controller.ts`: `createCandidate` → `CV_UPLOADED` (`source: "single"`). `bulkUploadCandidates` → `CV_UPLOADED` per successful row inside the loop (`source: "bulk"`, includes the CSV row number) — logged once per row, not once per request.
- `feedback.controller.ts`: `submitFeedback` → `FEEDBACK_SUBMITTED`. `updateFeedback` untouched — still exclusively `FeedbackAuditLog`, as decided.
- `interview.controller.ts`: `scheduleInterview` → `INTERVIEW_SCHEDULED`, plus `NOTIFICATION_SENT` per successfully-sent email (one per panelist, one for the candidate) — logged inside the existing try/catch, after each individual `sendEmail()` call succeeds, so a partial failure (e.g. one panelist's email bounces) is reflected accurately rather than logging one blanket "sent" event.
- `application.controller.ts`: `recordHiringDecision` → `NOTIFICATION_SENT` after the hire/reject email succeeds.
- New minimal read endpoint: `GET /api/audit-logs` (IT_ADMIN only, matches US-21/43's access restriction), supports `?action=`, `?entityType=`, `?from=`, `?to=` filters, capped at 200 rows. Reads only the new `AuditLog` table — does **not** merge in `ApplicationStageHistory` or `FeedbackAuditLog`. That three-table merge is still open (see the architecture entry above) and wasn't attempted here; this endpoint is only enough to verify the new logging actually works.
- **Default set without asking, flagging it:** notifications are logged only on successful send, not on failed attempts. Logging failures too is a reasonable future addition but wasn't part of what was asked for.

**Explicitly blocked, not built — no code path exists to hook into:** `ACCOUNT_CREATED`, `ACCOUNT_DEACTIVATED`, `ROLE_CHANGED`. Grepped every controller in the backend to confirm: there is no user-management or IT-Admin controller anywhere in the codebase. These three depend on that feature being built first (the actual IT Admin CRUD story, backlog rows 2/3) — add the corresponding `writeAuditLog()` calls to it once it exists.

**Migration run and verified (2026-08-27).** `npx prisma migrate dev` (migration `20260827094114_add_audit_log`) and `npx prisma generate` both run successfully on the real machine/DB. One real bug caught by the follow-up `tsc` pass (not pre-existing, mine): `writeAuditLog()`'s `metadata: metadata === undefined ? undefined : ...` tripped `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` -- explicitly assigning `undefined` isn't the same as omitting the key under that flag. Fixed with a spread (`...(metadata !== undefined ? { metadata } : {})`). After the fix, `tsc --noEmit` shows only the same pre-existing `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` errors in `candidate.controller.ts`, `vacancy.controller.ts`, `reports.controller.ts` that predate this session -- nothing new.

**End-to-end verified against the real backend/DB:** logged in as HR, created a vacancy via `POST /api/vacancies`, then confirmed via `GET /api/audit-logs` (as IT Admin) that a row was written -- `action: "VACANCY_CREATED"`, correct `entityId`, correct `metadata` (title/department), correct `user` nested (Hannah HR). Confirms the whole path works, not just that it type-checks.

**Regression coverage added.** New "12. AUDIT LOG (US-21/US-43)" section in `regression-test.ps1`: logs in as IT Admin, confirms a non-IT-Admin gets 403 from `GET /api/audit-logs`, triggers the two vacancy events not already exercised earlier in the script (edit, then close -- same vacancy created in section 2), then asserts one row exists for each of `VACANCY_CREATED`, `VACANCY_EDITED`, `VACANCY_CLOSED`, `CV_UPLOADED` (single-create path), `FEEDBACK_SUBMITTED`, `INTERVIEW_SCHEDULED`, and `NOTIFICATION_SENT` (2 rows for interview scheduling -- panelist + candidate email -- 1 row for the hiring decision email), matched by exact `entityId`/`entityType`, not just a count. Bulk-upload's `CV_UPLOADED` path is explicitly not covered here -- same reason bulk upload itself was never covered by this script: `Invoke-RestMethod` on this PowerShell version doesn't do multipart cleanly.

**REAL BUG — found and fixed on first actual run.** First run of the updated script: 38 pass, 22 fail, all 22 failures cascading from a single root cause -- "HR schedules INTERVIEW_1 with assigned panelist" (section 6) failed outright, and every check downstream that depended on that interview/application/feedback state failed as a direct consequence (stage progression, HM recommendation's stage-unchanged check, feedback submission, hiring decision, stage history count, KPIs, and 4 of the new audit-log checks). Sections 1-5 and the vacancy-only parts of section 12 passed independently, which pointed straight at `scheduleInterview` itself as the one broken thing, not a systemic issue.

Root cause: `INTERVIEW_SCHEDULED`'s `writeAuditLog()` call passed `scheduledAt: interview.scheduledAt` -- a raw `Date` object -- into the Json `metadata` field. Every other audit call in the codebase only ever passes strings/numbers/arrays; this was the one call site with a `Date`, and Prisma's runtime validation for `Json` input rejects `Date` objects (it's not a plain JSON-safe type). The call also wasn't wrapped in try/catch (unlike the email sends right after it), so it threw straight up to `scheduleInterview`'s outer catch and 500'd the whole request -- no interview ever got created, hence the full downstream cascade.

**Fix applied:** `scheduledAt: interview.scheduledAt.toISOString()` instead of the raw Date. Grepped every other `writeAuditLog()` call site to confirm this was the only one passing a non-JSON-safe value -- it was, and it's a real, worthwhile fix regardless of what follows below. `tsc --noEmit` clean on `interview.controller.ts` after the fix.

**Correction: that fix did NOT resolve the failure -- the actual cause was different, and I stated the Date theory with more confidence than I'd verified.** After a genuine backend restart, the exact same 22-check cascade persisted. Had the user reproduce just the failing call in isolation (login, create vacancy/candidate/application, assign interviewer to pool, schedule interview) so the real error would surface instead of being masked by the test script's blanket try/catch. The actual response: `409 -- "Scheduling conflict: Ian Interviewer already has another interview at this exact time"`. That's the US-11 conflict check working correctly, not a bug.

Real root cause: `regression-test.ps1`'s `$scheduledTime = "2026-09-10T10:00:00.000Z"` was a hardcoded literal -- every other piece of test data in the script (vacancy title, candidate email, etc.) is suffixed with `$suffix` to stay unique across re-runs, but this one wasn't. The DB isn't reset between regression runs, so the first run's interview for Ian Interviewer at that exact timestamp stayed in the database, and every subsequent run collided with it. This had nothing to do with the audit log work at all -- it's a pre-existing latent bug in the test script that simply never surfaced before because the script hadn't been run twice in a row against the same un-reset database until this session.

**Fix:** `$scheduledTime` now derived from `(Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().AddDays(14)` instead of a hardcoded string -- unique per run, same as everything else in the script. The other hardcoded interview time in the script (line ~130, the "panelist not in pool" test) doesn't need the same fix -- it uses a fake panelist id (999999) that fails validation before the time-conflict check is ever reached, so it can't collide across runs.

Re-run after that fix: interview scheduling passed, and 11 of the 12 new audit-log checks passed. The remaining 15 failures (sections 7/8/10/11, plus the hiring-decision `NOTIFICATION_SENT` check) were a single further cascade -- unrelated to audit logging and already known: `regression-test.ps1` still tested the old `PATCH /applications/:id/stage` endpoint, which was deliberately deleted earlier this project when the model changed to "HM's recommendation IS the decision" (see "Correction: HR-authority version above was itself superseded" above). That entry had already flagged `regression-test.ps1` as needing this exact update and it just hadn't been done yet.

**Rewrote sections 7 and 8 to match current behavior.** New "7. STAGE PROGRESSION VIA HM RECOMMENDATION" replaces the old direct-PATCH calls with three successive `POST /:id/recommendation` `ADVANCE` calls (SHORTLISTED -> INTERVIEW_1 -> INTERVIEW_2 -> FINAL_INTERVIEW), asserts HR and Interviewer both get 403 (HIRING_MANAGER only), asserts a 4th `ADVANCE` from `FINAL_INTERVIEW` is blocked with the "use the hiring decision endpoint" error (confirmed by reading `submitStageRecommendation` directly that this check runs before the `StageRecommendation` row is created, so the blocked call doesn't add to the count), and confirms exactly 3 recommendations are visible afterward. New "8. HM RECOMMENDATION -- REJECTION PATH" uses `app2` (idle at `APPLIED` from the earlier conflict test) to confirm `DO_NOT_PROGRESS` moves straight to `REJECTED`, and that a further recommendation on an already-`REJECTED` application is blocked. Also dropped the "no skip" framing entirely -- there's no endpoint left that accepts an arbitrary target stage, so skip-prevention is now structural, not a separate thing to test for.

**Re-run: 59/59 passed, 0 failed.** All 5 wired audit events, both permission checks, and the full binding-recommendation stage flow (advance x3, blocked 4th advance, rejection path, blocked recommendation on a finalized application) verified against the real backend/DB. This closes out both the audit log build-out and the stale `regression-test.ps1` gap from the earlier stage-authority redesign in the same pass.

## Wireframe audit: Leadership Management / Management / Hiring Manager dashboards (13 screens)

Checked all 13 wireframes against the actual decided model before any frontend work starts. Found real conflicts, not just styling gaps:

**Pipeline stages shown are wrong across every dashboard.** All three role tiers used a fixed 6-bucket funnel (CV Screening -> Phone Screening -> Technical Interview -> Final Interview -> Offer -> Hired). Doesn't match the actual model (`APPLIED, SHORTLISTED, [HR-configurable rounds], FINAL_INTERVIEW, HIRED, REJECTED`), and can't match it as a single fixed global list -- US-05's resolution makes interview-round labels per-vacancy free text, aggregated by rank, never by label. **Fix:** replace named buckets with rank-based generic ones (Shortlisted, Round 1, Round 2, ..., Final round, Hired, Rejected) everywhere a pipeline widget appears.

**Rejected candidates were missing from every pipeline/KPI display**, despite `REJECTED` being a first-class terminal stage and `getKpis` already returning a rejection count. Add it wherever Hired is shown.

**Hiring Manager displayed as a vacancy-level attribute in 3 places** (Management's "Department Vacancies" table's HM column; HM's own "My Vacancies" nav item and "My Open Vacancies" tile). Contradicts the explicit earlier decision that `hiringManagerId` lives on `CandidateApplication`, not `Vacancy`, specifically because one vacancy can have different HMs across different candidates. **Fix:** show HMs plural per vacancy, or move the display to application-level.

**Offer stage decided: remove it, don't add scope.** Multiple screens had an "Offer" pipeline bucket, a "3 offers await responses" attention item, and an "Offers: 31" KPI tile. Nothing in any doc has an offer-extended/accepted/declined state -- the real flow goes straight from `FINAL_INTERVIEW` to `HIRED` via the hiring decision endpoint. User confirmed: remove from wireframes, don't add the feature.

**Categorical feedback labels decided: numeric-score-only, don't revive the removed field.** HM's Candidate Comparison and Pending Decisions screens used "Strong Hire / Hire / Further Review / Do Not Hire / Pending" labels. This is functionally the same shape as `Feedback.recommendation` (`HIRE, REJECT, NEXT_STAGE, HOLD`), which was deliberately removed earlier this project specifically because it competed with the real hiring decision as a second source of truth. User confirmed: wireframes should show the average numeric score + written comments only, matching what `compareApplicationsForVacancy` actually returns today. No schema change.

**Backend gaps these wireframes assume exist, flagged but not yet built:**
- Department-level KPI aggregation (fill rate, fastest hiring, most open roles by department) -- buildable off existing `Vacancy.department`, not built.
- Month-by-month historical trend data (Leadership's "Hiring Trends" chart) -- this is US-49's dashboard, one of the AC stories still sitting on a placeholder edge case from earlier this session. No trend-over-time endpoint exists.
- Excel export -- only PDF export exists today; wireframes offered "PDF / Excel" on every report screen.
- In-app notifications list/unread count -- bell icon with a badge appears on every screen; US-50 decided in-app-only scope but zero backend exists for it.

Not yet applied to the actual wireframe files or built into the backend -- this entry records the audit findings and the two scope decisions (no Offer, no categorical feedback) so they're not re-litigated later.

## CV upload rebuilt: real file upload with name-extraction and review step (US-06/US-07 merged)

**Problem found:** neither existing candidate-creation endpoint actually does what the AC says. Checked the real AC text directly -- US-06 ("Individual CV upload") and US-07 ("Bulk CV upload") both explicitly describe uploading actual CV files with format validation ("Can select and upload one/multiple CV file(s)... Supported file formats are validated upon upload... Uploading a CV in a non-supported format displays an error message"). What's built: `createCandidate` takes `cvUrl` as a plain string (no file involved at all), and `bulkUploadCandidates` uploads a CSV of metadata rows (also not CV files). Item #20's original decision ("real CSV file upload over pasting JSON") was a CSV-of-metadata design that quietly diverged from the AC and was never reconciled until now.

**Decision: merge US-06 and US-07 into one story.** One "Upload" control, HR selects one or more actual PDF files, same code path handles 1-to-N (same reasoning as why the CSV bulk endpoint already generalized down to a single row fine). Delete US-06 and US-07 as separate backlog/AC rows; replace with one merged story.

**Decision: auto-extract name/email/phone from the PDF content**, rather than HR typing it in per file. Real, non-trivial feature -- needs actual text extraction from the PDF (not just storing the file), then pattern-matching/heuristics to pull out email (reliable, structured format), phone (reasonably reliable), and name (the hard one -- free-form resume text has no reliable pattern, real risk of grabbing "Curriculum Vitae," a template header, or a company name instead of the candidate's actual name).

**Decision: PDF only for now.** DOC/DOCX explicitly out of scope for this pass -- can be added later without a rebuild, just a second extraction path.

**Decision: HR reviews and can fix the extracted data before it's saved as the real candidate record.** Not fully automatic. Directly matters because the email address extracted here is what every later feature depends on -- interview-scheduling emails and hire/reject emails (items #31/#32) send to `Candidate.email` with no other way to reach the candidate. A silently-wrong extracted email means those emails go nowhere and nobody notices until someone asks why a candidate never showed up. So the flow is: HR selects file(s) -> system extracts name/email/phone per file -> HR sees a pre-filled, editable form per file -> HR confirms (or corrects) -> record is created.

**Decision: rename the stored PDF to the (confirmed) candidate name.** Cosmetic/organizational, low-risk, agreed alongside the above -- happens after HR confirms the name, not before (renaming off an unconfirmed extraction would just propagate the same risk into the filename).

**Not yet implemented.** This replaces both `createCandidate` and `bulkUploadCandidates`'s current mechanics -- real scope, not a relabel:
- File storage strategy not yet decided (local disk under `backend/` vs cloud storage) -- same open question as AWS hosting, worth deciding together.
- PDF text extraction library not yet chosen (Node-side, e.g. `pdf-parse` or similar -- needs to run in the actual backend, not the sandbox used for wireframe mockups).
- Name/email/phone extraction heuristics not yet written.
- New two-step endpoint flow needed: (1) upload + extract (returns extracted data per file, doesn't create records yet), (2) confirm (HR-edited data actually creates the candidate record(s) and renames the stored file). Single-file and multi-file both go through the same flow.
- `Candidate.cvUrl` (currently a plain external-URL-shaped string) needs to become an internally-stored file reference/path instead.
- Product Backlog and Acceptance Criteria docs still need updating to reflect the merged story (delete US-06/US-07 as separate rows, add the merged one with the review-step and PDF-only scope spelled out).

**Sequencing decided:** no dependency forces this to wait for backend "completion" -- it sits in the same open queue as the US-05 stage catalog, IT Admin accounts, and duplicate detection. Can be built whenever, in whatever order.

**Storage/parsing decisions:** local disk for now, cloud later -- behind a single storage module (`saveFile()`/`getFile()`/`deleteFile()`, same choke-point pattern as `writeAuditLog()`/`transitionApplicationStage()`/`sendEmail()`) so the later swap only touches one file. CVs served via an authenticated endpoint (`GET /api/candidates/:id/cv`), never a public static folder -- same category of risk as the passwordHash-leak bug found earlier this session. Renamed file gets the candidate's real DB id appended (`John_Doe_142.pdf`) to prevent two same-named candidates from silently overwriting each other's file. PDF parsing: `pdf-parse` (pure JS, no native binaries, matches the reasoning already used to pick `pdfkit` for report export). Known limitation, not blocking: scanned/image-only PDFs with no text layer return empty extraction -- degrades gracefully into the review step already decided (HR just fills the form manually), not worth OCR infrastructure for this project.

## Candidates screen: row scope resolved (US-13 vs US-47 surfaced again in a real wireframe)

User uploaded a wireframe for a global, top-level "Candidates" nav screen (Search/Stage/Status filters, Candidate ID/Candidate/CV/Stage/Status columns, "Upload CV" button). Flagged a real gap: no Vacancy column or filter, but `stage` is stored per-`CandidateApplication`, not per-candidate -- doesn't work once a candidate has applied to more than one vacancy, which row's stage would show?

**Decision: one row per candidate-application, not per candidate.** Add a Vacancy column. A candidate with 2 applications appears as 2 rows, one per application, each showing that specific application's stage/status -- matches how the data is actually stored, no new schema needed.

**Status column confirmed correct as designed**, not a duplicate of Stage -- maps to US-09's AC: a 3-state marker (green = shortlisted, red = rejected, yellow = unreviewed), derived from the same `stage` field rather than a second stored value. No schema change needed, just correct mapping logic (yellow if `APPLIED`, red if `REJECTED`, green otherwise) when the screen is built.

## CV upload backend built (US-06/US-07)

Built the real-file-upload flow specified above. No Prisma migration needed -- `Candidate.cvUrl` was already a plain string column, just repurposed to hold an internal filename instead of an external URL.

- `backend/src/utils/fileStorage.ts` (new): `saveFile`/`getFile`/`fileExists`/`renameFile`/`deleteFile`/`sanitizeForFilename`, all scoped to `<cwd>/uploads/cvs`, single choke point for the later local-to-cloud swap.
- `backend/src/utils/cvExtraction.ts` (new): uses `pdf-parse` v2's `PDFParse` class (`new PDFParse({ data: buffer }).getText()` -- v2 replaced the old default-export-function API from v1, worth knowing if anyone looks this up later). Regex email/phone extraction, best-effort name heuristic (first plausible line in the first 8 non-empty lines, skipping generic headers and anything matching the email/phone regex).
- `POST /api/candidates/cv-extract` (HR only, `multer().array("files")`, PDF-mimetype validated): saves every uploaded file to disk immediately under a `randomUUID()` filename, best-effort extracts name/email/phone per file, creates no `Candidate` rows yet. Handles both individual and bulk upload in one endpoint -- satisfies the US-06/US-07 merge without a separate bulk code path.
- `POST /api/candidates/cv-confirm` (HR only, JSON body): takes HR-reviewed/corrected `{fileId, name, email, phoneNumber}` per file, creates the `Candidate` row, renames the stored file to `<candidateId>_<sanitized name>.pdf` to guarantee no collisions between same-named candidates, writes a `CV_UPLOADED` audit log entry (`source: "file"`).
- `GET /api/candidates/:id/cv` (any authenticated role): streams the stored PDF. Deliberately not a public `express.static()` folder -- same reasoning as the passwordHash/resetToken leak fixed earlier this project.

**Scoping choice made without re-confirming, flagging here rather than assuming it's forgotten:** the old `createCandidate` (JSON, single) and `bulkUploadCandidates` (CSV, bulk) endpoints were left in place untouched, running in parallel with the new file-upload endpoints. Reason: `regression-test.ps1` and other code depend on them, and retiring them is a separate decision (does HR still need a metadata-only path for candidates without a CV on file yet?) that hasn't been asked. Worth a deliberate call on whether to deprecate them now that real upload exists.

**Known gap, not addressed here:** automated regression coverage for the two new endpoints hits the same multipart-upload limitation already noted for the original bulk CSV upload (item #20) -- `Invoke-RestMethod` on the project's PowerShell version doesn't do multipart cleanly. Manual testing only for now, consistent with how bulk CSV upload has always been tested.

`tsc --noEmit` run clean on all new code (`fileStorage.ts`, `cvExtraction.ts`, and the three new controller functions) -- the only remaining type errors in the codebase are the pre-existing `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` ones in `createCandidate`/`bulkUploadCandidates`/`updateCandidate`/`vacancy.controller.ts`/`reports.controller.ts`, unrelated to this change and already flagged in earlier entries.

## Old candidate JSON/CSV endpoints deleted -- full replacement decided and executed

User pushed back on leaving the old endpoints in place, asking if full replacement wasn't "obvious." Answer: the endpoints themselves were safe to delete immediately -- no AC story actually calls for metadata-only candidate entry, US-06/US-07 both specify real file upload, and no frontend code depends on the old routes yet. The real cost wasn't the endpoints, it was `regression-test.ps1`: almost every downstream section (applications, interviews, feedback, hiring decisions) needs a candidate to exist, and the only tested way to create one was the endpoint about to be deleted, and the real replacement path requires multipart upload, which this project's `Invoke-RestMethod` can't do cleanly on Windows PowerShell 5.1.

**Resolved without waiting on that limitation.** `confirmCvUpload` doesn't actually require the file to have arrived via multipart -- it only checks the file exists on disk at the given `fileId` path. So the regression script now seeds test candidates by copying a fixture PDF (`regression-fixtures/sample-cv.pdf`, generated with `pdfkit` -- same library already used for report export) directly into `backend/uploads/cvs/` and calling `POST /candidates/cv-confirm` with a generated `fileId`. This exercises the real create/rename/audit-log logic in `confirmCvUpload`, needs no multipart support, and works on any PowerShell version.

**Additionally, real multipart is now tested where possible.** The script checks `$PSVersionTable.PSVersion.Major -ge 6` and, when true, calls `POST /candidates/cv-extract` with `-Form` (supported since PowerShell 6.1/Core) using the same fixture PDF, verifies the extracted name/email/phone match the fixture's known embedded text exactly, confirms that upload through `cv-confirm`, and checks the CV download endpoint returns real PDF bytes. On Windows PowerShell 5.1 this block is skipped with an explicit message rather than silently passing.

**Endpoint removal also closed a real gap, not just cleanup.** `updateCandidate` used to accept an arbitrary `cvUrl` string from the client and write it straight to the DB column that `downloadCv` later reads from disk via `path.join(CV_DIR, filename)`. That's a path-traversal vector (`../../etc/passwd`-shaped input). Fixed two ways: `cvUrl` removed from `updateCandidate`'s accepted fields entirely (it's only ever set by `confirmCvUpload` now), and `fileStorage.ts` now resolves and verifies every path stays inside `CV_DIR` before any read/write/rename/delete, as defense in depth for any future code path that touches these functions.

Also fixed while in this code: `updateCandidate`'s pre-existing `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` violation (same spread-pattern fix as `writeAuditLog` earlier this session) -- `tsc --noEmit` is now fully clean on `candidate.controller.ts`. Remaining type errors in the codebase are unrelated, pre-existing ones in `reports.controller.ts` and `vacancy.controller.ts`.

`tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors in any file touched this round. Regression script changes have not yet been run against the real backend -- next step is for the user to run it locally (no new migration needed, no schema changes this round).

## Real bug found: pdf-parse crashed the entire backend on startup (Windows + Node 24)

User ran the updated regression script and every single check failed, including plain HR login -- something unrelated to any code touched this session. Backend terminal showed the actual cause: the server process was crashing on startup with `ReferenceError: DOMMatrix is not defined`, thrown from inside `pdfjs-dist/legacy/build/pdf.mjs`.

**Root cause.** `pdf-parse@2.4.5` pulls in `pdfjs-dist`, which pulls in the optional native binding `@napi-rs/canvas` to polyfill browser-only globals (`DOMMatrix`, `ImageData`, `Path2D`) that its Node build still references at module-import time. On this machine (Windows, Node v24.18.0) the native binding failed to load ("Failed to load native binding"), the fallback polyfill silently no-ops instead of defining the globals, and then `pdfjs-dist` unconditionally executes `new DOMMatrix()` at the top of the module -- crashing the whole process before a single request can be handled. This reproduced cleanly by disabling the matching `@napi-rs/canvas` platform package in a sandbox and confirming the identical crash.

**This directly contradicts the reasoning used to pick pdf-parse in the first place** ("pure JS, no native binaries" -- see the CV-upload rebuild entry above). That reasoning was wrong for v2 specifically; it has an optional native dependency after all, it just doesn't fail loudly when missing, it fails by crashing at import time instead. Worth remembering for future dependency choices: "no native deps" needs to be checked against the actual installed tree (`npm ls --all`), not assumed from a library's reputation.

**Alternatives tried before landing on a fix:** `pdf2json` (zero dependencies, but failed to parse the pdfkit-generated fixture with `Invalid XRef stream header` -- its parser doesn't handle the cross-reference stream format modern pdfkit outputs) and `pdf-parse@1.1.1` (the old pre-rewrite version, vendors pdf.js v1.10.100 from ~2018, which also failed on the same fixture with `bad XRef entry` for the same reason). Both would have meant either a worse/older parser or an unverified regression against real-world PDFs, so neither was the right fix.

**Actual fix: `backend/src/pdfEnvPolyfills.ts`.** A tiny side-effect-only module that defines minimal no-op `DOMMatrix`/`ImageData`/`Path2D` globals (only if not already present) before anything else runs. Imported as the very first line of `server.ts`, ahead of `dotenv/config` and `app.js` -- ES module evaluation order guarantees this runs before the `candidate.routes -> candidate.controller -> cvExtraction -> pdf-parse` chain gets evaluated, regardless of how deep that import graph is. These globals are never used for anything real -- CV parsing only ever calls `getText()`, never any canvas-based rendering (`getImage()`/`getScreenshot()`), so stand-in classes are sufficient. Verified in a sandbox with the native canvas binding deliberately disabled: identical crash reproduced, then resolved by this fix, with text/name/email/phone extraction still correct on the same fixture PDF that pdf2json and pdf-parse@1 both failed to even parse.

**Confirmed fixed on the real machine.** User restarted the backend and re-ran `regression-test.ps1`: 61/61 passed, 0 failed (up from 59 -- the two new 404 checks for the deleted endpoints). Confirmed on Windows PowerShell 5.1, so the real multipart `cv-extract` sub-block correctly self-skipped with its explicit message rather than silently passing.

One more real finding fell out of the passing run for free: the dev server's console output showed every email as `===== DEV EMAIL (not actually sent) =====`. This confirms US-39 (Gmail integration) status should be **Not Built**, not the "Unverified" hedge used earlier today -- `sendEmail()` is fully wired into every flow that needs it, but the actual transport is a console log, not real SMTP. Updated `docs/story-implementation-table.md` and `docs/Story-Implementation-Table.docx` accordingly, and also corrected the now-stale US-06/US-07 notes that still said the old endpoints "exist in parallel" (they were deleted in the entry above).
