## Interviewer screens -- audit against "Corrections that should be made to the frontend.docx", first pass (My Interviews)

User uploaded a large master corrections document (also the source of the earlier HR Interviews module and Follow Ups checklist-style asks) and asked to continue from its Interviewer section. Extracted the doc's embedded reference screenshots for that section to compare against the current build rather than going off the text alone.

**Found and fixed, unambiguous:**
- `MyInterviewsPage.css`'s `.miv-page` had the same `max-width: 1000px` fixed-cap bug already fixed on every HR page this session -- changed to `90vw`. Table also had no `table-layout: fixed`/explicit column widths, so added the same column-percentage + last-column-right-aligned/second-column-centered treatment used on the Follow Ups tables (`.miv-col-date/time/stage/position/candidate/feedback`, summing to 100%).
- Added a Submitted/Pending/All filter bar above the table (client-side, filtering on the `feedbackSubmitted` boolean the API already returns -- no backend change needed for this one).
- **Real backend bug found and fixed**, not just cosmetic: `interviewer.controller.ts`'s `listMyCandidates` built its Prisma `where` clause as `interviews: { some: { panelists: { some: { userId } } } }` -- but `panelists` doesn't exist on `Interview`, it lives on `InterviewSlot` (`Interview.slot.panelists`), per the InterviewSlot/Interview schema split done earlier this project. This slipped through `tsc` because the `where` variable is typed `any`, so TypeScript never checked the shape against Prisma's generated types. At runtime this would throw a Prisma validation error for every interviewer loading "My Candidates" (or silently misbehave, depending on Prisma's exact validation behavior) -- either way, the endpoint was broken on the current schema. Fixed to `interviews: { some: { slot: { panelists: { some: { userId } } } } }`. This one couldn't be verified live (no running app/DB in this environment) -- flagged to the user to confirm "My Candidates" actually loads data after this fix.
- Checked the previously-flagged "log out button at the bottom of the candidate list instead of the sidebar" complaint from the doc: already correct in the current `InterviewerLayout.tsx`/`.css` -- the logout button lives in the `<aside>` outside the scrolling `<main>` content area, so it's always pinned regardless of candidate-list length. No action needed; this was likely fixed in an earlier pass or the doc's complaint predates the current layout.

**Flagged the contradiction with an earlier locked decision, then built it once confirmed.** The doc's screenshots show "My Candidates" restructured from a flat all-candidates table into a landing page listing vacancy+stage groups that drill into that group's candidates -- directly reversing an explicit earlier decision ("every candidate-application this interviewer has ever sat on a panel for... all-time, regardless of outcome" / "Status here is NOT a separate concept"). Asked the user before touching it (two-part question: build the restructure at all, and whether a landing row is one vacancy or one vacancy+stage pair) rather than guess and risk a throwaway rebuild. User confirmed: yes, build it, and group by vacancy+stage.

**Implementation:**
- New backend endpoint `GET /interviewer/vacancy-stages` (`listMyVacancyStages` in `interviewer.controller.ts`): queries `InterviewPanelist` for this user, groups by `vacancyStageId` (not `vacancyId` -- the same interviewer can show up as separate rows for different rounds of the same vacancy), returns `{ vacancyId, vacancyTitle, vacancyStageId, vacancyStageName, vacancyStageOrder, candidateCount }`. `candidateCount` is the distinct count of `applicationId`s across every slot in that group (a stage can have multiple time slots, e.g. a morning and afternoon batch of the same round).
- `listMyCandidates` gained an optional `vacancyStageId` filter, scoping to "candidates I actually interviewed at this specific round" -- deliberately not filtered by the candidate's *current* stage, since a candidate may have since advanced to round 2 but still belongs in this interviewer's round-1 history. The old comment describing this as the primary "My Candidates" data source was stale (it's now only reached with `vacancyStageId` set) and was corrected; the unscoped mode is kept working as a general-purpose query, just no longer used by the frontend.
- Frontend split into two pages: `MyCandidatesPage.tsx` (landing, list of "{Vacancy} - {Stage}" rows with a candidate count, from `listMyVacancyStages()`) and a new `MyCandidatesGroupPage.tsx` (the old flat-table logic, now scoped via the route's `:vacancyStageId` param, with a back link and the group name as the page heading). New route `/interviewer/candidates/:vacancyStageId` added in `App.tsx`.
- Status simplified per the doc ("make status in progress", "remove the status filter"): dropped the "All Statuses" filter entirely; kept the "All Stages" (application recruitment-stage: Applied/Shortlisted/Hired/Rejected) filter and column unchanged, since the doc only asked to remove the Status one. Judgment call on the label itself: gave HIRED its own "Hired" badge instead of folding it into "In Progress" alongside SHORTLISTED, since a hired candidate reads as concluded, not ongoing -- flagged to the user in chat in case they wanted HIRED folded in too.
- Also fixed on the same page: `.myc-page`'s `max-width: 1000px` → `90vw` (same fixed-cap bug as everywhere else this session), and the candidate table given `table-layout: fixed` + explicit column widths + right-aligned last column, matching the pattern used on Follow Ups/My Interviews.

**Follow-up round after live screenshots (My Interviews, My Candidates group page, Feedback page):**

- `My Interviews`: rows now sort pending-to-top (soonest-scheduled first, since those still need action) then submitted-to-bottom (most-recently-commented first). Needed `feedbackSubmittedAt` added to `GET /interviews/mine`'s response (`interview.controller.ts`'s `listMyInterviews` -- feedback query now selects `createdAt`, not just `id`) since the frontend had no timestamp to sort submitted rows by before this.
- `My Candidates` group page: replaced the "All Stages" (RecruitmentStage: Applied/Shortlisted/Hired/Rejected) filter with a "Vacancy - Stage" quick-switch dropdown (populated from the same `listMyVacancyStages()` call already used for the page's own heading/back-link lookup) that navigates between groups without going back to the landing page. Added a genuinely new Status filter (In Progress / Hired / Rejected, matching the `statusFor()` labels from the previous pass) that filters the table client-side. Dropped the "Stage" column from the table entirely -- with the Status filter/column now covering that ground and the page already scoped to one specific interview stage, showing the raw `Applied/Shortlisted/Hired/Rejected` value in a separate column was redundant. Table column CSS widths adjusted for 4 columns instead of 5.
- `Feedback` page: replaced the cramped single ~640px-wide column (leaving most of the screen empty) with a two-column layout -- form on the left, a new "Interview Details" info card on the right (candidate, vacancy, stage, scheduled time, panel list) using space that was previously just blank rather than adding anything not already available from the `InterviewDetail` the page already fetches.
- **Investigated but did not change:** the "reason for change" field showing up on what the user described as a first-time submission. Re-read `FeedbackPage.tsx`'s `isEditMode = existing !== null` and the backend's `getMyFeedbackForInterview` (scoped to "this interviewer's own feedback on this interview") -- the logic already matches the rule as described: it only requires a reason if a `Feedback` row already exists for that interviewer+interview pair. No code path found that would set `existing` non-null before a first save. Flagged to the user in chat to confirm whether feedback had already been submitted for that specific interview earlier (e.g. during prior testing), since the current code shouldn't be able to demand a reason on a genuine first save.
- **Not built, flagged only:** deleting test `Candidate` rows before the client demo will not by itself make the next real upload get ID 1 / `0001` -- MySQL/MariaDB's `AUTO_INCREMENT` counter does not reset on `DELETE`, only on `TRUNCATE` (which itself requires clearing every FK-dependent row first, same as `delete-candidate.ts`'s cleanup order). Explained this to the user in chat rather than building an unrequested wipe-and-reset script, since they said they'd be doing the cleanup themselves before the demo -- offered to write one if wanted.

**Tiny follow-up on the Feedback page, plus a real sidebar-label bug found in the same pass:**

- `Feedback` page: widened `.fb-page` to `90vw` (was `1000px`) and widened the `.fb-layout` grid's second column from `300px`→`360px` with a bigger gap (`40px`→`64px`) so the Interview Details card actually reaches the right edge instead of stopping partway across -- the card is the grid's second column, so growing the page automatically pushes it right regardless of the form column's own `max-width`. Also gave the info card more internal padding/row spacing and made the comments textarea taller (`rows={8}`→`12`) to fill the vertical space that was previously empty.
- Sidebar brand label: `InterviewerLayout.tsx` said "Altrium HR" in its sidebar header, which is wrong outside the actual HR module -- changed to just "Altrium" for that layout. Flagged (not fixed) that `HMLayout.tsx`, `MgmtLayout.tsx`, and `LeadershipLayout.tsx` all have the exact same "Altrium HR" label and are still pending their own corrections-doc review (tasks #33-35) -- same one-line fix will apply there.

## Hiring Manager screens fixes (corrections doc, task #33)

Audited the corrections doc's Hiring Manager section (Dashboard, Vacancies, Candidate Comparison, Pending Decisions) against current code the same way the Interviewer section was done: extracted the doc's before/after wireframe screenshots for this section and diffed them against the live pages.

**Real gap found and confirmed before building:** the doc's wireframe has a Vacancies → click a vacancy → full candidate list → click a candidate → decision page flow. That drill-down didn't exist at all -- `VacanciesPage` rows weren't clickable and there was no per-vacancy candidate list endpoint. The only existing way to act on a candidate was the flat `Pending Decisions` queue (which already had full Proceed/Do Not Proceed/Hire/Reject logic, just reached from one place). Asked the user via `AskUserQuestion` before building a new endpoint + 2 new pages rather than assume; confirmed: build it.

**Backend (`hiringManager.controller.ts` / `application.controller.ts`):**
- Extracted `isReadyForDecision(currentRoundInterviews)` from the inline logic that used to live only inside `getPendingDecisionsForHm`'s loop -- now shared by that function and the new per-application endpoint below, so the flat queue and the drill-down page can never disagree about when a decision is actually actionable (interview happened + every assigned panelist's feedback is in).
- New `getVacancyCandidates` (`GET /hiring-manager/vacancies/:vacancyId/candidates`): lists every candidate on a vacancy this HM has access to -- deliberately broad (not filtered to `hiringManagerId = me`), matching the existing broad scoping already used for `getMyVacancies`/Recruitment Progress, since this is a browsing view rather than a personal action queue like Pending Decisions.
- New `getApplicationForDecision` (`GET /hiring-manager/applications/:id/decision`): generalizes the Pending-Decisions per-application view to work for any candidate on an accessible vacancy, not just ones currently in the ready queue -- returns `awaitingDecision: false` with read-only context (current round, stage, or final HIRED/REJECTED outcome) for candidates who aren't there yet, instead of 404ing or omitting them.
- `getMyVacancies` now also returns `allDecided` (every application on that vacancy has reached HIRED/REJECTED) -- used client-side to sink fully-decided vacancies to the bottom of the Vacancies list per the doc ("if they make all the decisions for all the candidates in a certain vacancy move that to the bottom of the list").
- **Comments field wired up, not added new:** the doc's wireframe shows an "Add Comments" box paired with the Proceed/Do Not Proceed and Hire/Reject actions. `StageRecommendation.comments` already existed as a schema column but `submitStageRecommendation` never read `comments` from the request body -- wired it through now (zero migration needed). `recordHiringDecision` (HIRE/REJECT) has no equivalent dedicated column, so its optional `comments` is logged via the existing generic `AuditLog` instead (new `HM_DECISION_COMMENT` action, same pattern as `NOTIFICATION_SENT` tracking) rather than adding a schema field for one text note.
- Noted but did not fix: neither `recordHiringDecision` nor `submitStageRecommendation` actually check that the acting HM owns the application (`hiringManagerId`) -- any authenticated HIRING_MANAGER can currently hit either endpoint for any application ID. Pre-existing gap, unrelated to this pass; flagging for a future security-hardening pass rather than fixing opportunistically mid-feature.

**Frontend:**
- `VacanciesPage.tsx`: added Date Range (All time/Last 30/Last 90 days, filters on `createdAt`)/Department/Vacancy/Status filter selects plus an explicit Apply button (matches the wireframe's button, filters are staged in local state and only take effect on click rather than filtering live) above the table. Rows are now clickable, navigating to the new `VacancyCandidatesPage`. Rows sort with `allDecided` vacancies always last, then by status severity (Overdue → Delayed → On track) among the rest. Judgment call: the Status filter offers On track/Delayed/Overdue (three values) even though the doc's text only asked for "on track or overdue" -- kept Delayed since it already existed and is meaningful, rather than deleting an existing distinction to match a simplified two-value description.
- New `VacancyCandidatesPage.tsx` (`/hiring-manager/vacancies/:vacancyId/candidates`): candidate table for one vacancy (name/email, current round, status badge, an "Awaiting your decision" flag), rows clickable into the new decision page.
- New `CandidateDecisionPage.tsx` (`/hiring-manager/applications/:applicationId`, routed page not a modal -- same "convert modal to routed page" pattern as the HR Candidate Detail page, task #42): renders feedback grouped into "Stage 0X" blocks with one card per interviewer (name/score/feedback text) matching the wireframe, an Add Comments textarea, and Proceed/Do Not Proceed or Hire/Reject depending on `isFinalRound` -- but only when `awaitingDecision` is true. Otherwise shows a read-only state (already-decided outcome, or "waiting on the panel's feedback") with just a Back button.
- `PendingDecisionsPage.tsx`: added the same Add Comments textarea to the existing review modal for consistency (comments now flow to the backend from both places), reset on modal close. `.pd-page` widened to `90vw`; table given `table-layout: fixed` + explicit column widths + right-aligned last column, matching the established pattern elsewhere.
- `DashboardPage.tsx`: Recruitment Progress and Needs Attention are now a two-column grid (`.hmd-lower-grid`, side-by-side/"parallel" per the wireframe) instead of two stacked full-width sections; Needs Attention now always renders (with an empty state) instead of only appearing when `awaitingMyDecision > 0`, so the two-column layout doesn't collapse to one column when there's nothing pending. Split the KPI-tile and Recruitment-Progress-tile number styling into separate classes (`.hmd-kpi-value` at 34px vs new `.hmd-progress-value` at 22px) so the 4 headline stats read as more prominent than the progress breakdown, per the doc ("a little bigger comparatively but still enough to be seen, not too big") -- previously both sections shared one class and were visually identical.
- `CandidateComparisonPage.tsx`: restructured `.cc-panels` from a flat 2-column grid (Ranking | Distribution side-by-side, Comments full-width below) into Ranking+Distribution stacked in a left column with Comments as a right column matching its height, per the wireframe. Bumped the ranking-row rank/score text sizes up slightly for legibility.
- All four pages' page-wrapper widened to `90vw` (same fixed-cap bug as every other screen this session).

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend after this pass.

**Demo data:** added `backend/scripts/seed-hm-demo.ts` (idempotent, `npx tsx scripts/seed-hm-demo.ts`) covering all 4 HM screens for `hiringmanager@altrium.test` -- 4 vacancies spanning every candidate state used by the code above (mid-round ready-for-decision, mid-round not-ready, partial-panel-feedback, final-round ready, already-hired, fresh-applied, 5 scored candidates for Comparison, and one fully-decided vacancy to confirm it sinks to the bottom of the list).

**Dashboard follow-up round, from live screenshots against the wireframe:**

- KPI tiles (Open Vacancies/Awaiting My Decision/Hired/Rejected): the wireframe has the label READ FIRST with the number landing big underneath, as the visual hook of the page -- previous build had number-then-label (backwards) and had the number too small to be the focal point. Swapped the JSX order and bumped `.hmd-kpi-value` from 34px to 44px. Refactored both `.hmd-kpi-tile` and `.hmd-progress-tile` to `display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap` instead of relying on `margin-top` on the label, since that margin was shared via one `.hmd-kpi-label` class used by both tile types with opposite label/value orders -- a margin tweak for one would have broken spacing on the other.
- Recruitment Progress tiles: switched `.hmd-progress-grid` from a 4-column CSS grid to `display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;justify-content:center` with each tile at a fixed `flex: 0 0 calc(25% - 12px)` -- with a fixed grid, an incomplete last row (e.g. 2 tiles left over) stays left-aligned; with flex-wrap + justify-content:center, a same-size incomplete row centers itself automatically. Bumped `.hmd-progress-value` 22px -> 27px ("a little bigger") and increased gaps/padding throughout the lower section per "spaced out more, more to the bottom" (`.hmd-lower-grid` gap 24->32, `.hmd-progress-grid` gap 12->16, tile padding 16->20px, `.hmd-attention` padding 16 20->20 24).
- Needs Attention: this was still just one generic aggregate line ("You have N candidates awaiting your decision"). The wireframe shows several distinct bullet types (compare candidates / submit a specific candidate's decision / follow up on missing feedback) -- built that out for real rather than faking the wireframe's placeholder names. `getMyDashboard` (`hiringManager.controller.ts`) now returns `attentionItems: {type, label, link}[]` combining three sources, all scoped narrowly (`hiringManagerId = me`, same personal-ownership scoping as Awaiting My Decision/Hired/Rejected, not the broad org-wide scoping Recruitment Progress uses): (1) one bullet per candidate actually in the ready-for-decision queue (reuses `getPendingDecisionsForHm`'s rows, itemized by name instead of collapsed into a count, linking straight to that candidate's new decision page), (2) vacancies with >= 3 shortlisted candidates ("Compare N vacancy candidates", linking to Candidate Comparison with `?vacancyId=` pre-selecting that vacancy -- `CandidateComparisonPage` now reads that query param via `useSearchParams` on load), (3) a count of interviews that happened but still have a panelist's feedback missing ("Follow up on N missing feedback forms" -- plain text, no link, since there's no HM-facing page that acts on this directly). Threshold for (2) is `COMPARE_NUDGE_THRESHOLD = 3`, an implementation default like the existing `DELAYED_WARNING_WINDOW_DAYS` pattern, not a separately confirmed decision.

## Management screens fixes (corrections doc, task #34)

**Reversed a previously locked scope decision -- confirmed with the user first.** `management.controller.ts` had an explicit code comment locking this as "the minimal KPI screen... not the full 5-tab Management wireframe set" and deferring Department Vacancies/Candidate Progress/Upcoming Interviews/Reports to "Sprint 2, per the locked backlog scope." The corrections doc's wireframes show all 5 as if they should exist now. Used `AskUserQuestion` before touching anything (same pattern as the Interviewer My Candidates restructure and the HM drill-down) rather than silently reversing an explicit earlier decision: confirmed build all 4 missing pages, and confirmed "View Report"/"Export" should be full generated PDFs (not a lighter in-app-table + CSV option that was also offered).

**Wireframe inconsistency, resolved by trusting content over headers.** The doc has two different draft wireframes for the Management sidebar: an early one-off mockup labels the tabs "Recruitment Progress / Department Performance / Hiring Trends / Export Reports" with no page content shown; a later, fully-detailed set of 4 actual page mockups consistently uses "Department Vacancies / Candidate Progress / Upcoming Interviews / Reports" and shows real tables. Treated the detailed, internally-consistent set as authoritative and ignored the abandoned early draft's naming. Similarly, the Upcoming Interviews wireframe's table header row literally says "Days at Stage" in the 4th column but every sample row under it is a vacancy name ("Network Engineer", "IT Assistant" -- clearly a copy-paste header left over from the Candidate Progress table above it in the doc) -- built that column as "Vacancy" to match the actual data shown, not the mislabeled header.

**Backend (all new, `management.controller.ts` + `backend/src/utils/pdfReport.ts`):**
- Every endpoint is scoped by `req.user!.department` (Management's own department, from the existing "Department Recruitment" framing) -- there's no multi-department switcher anywhere in the wireframes, so unlike the HM screens' Department filter dropdown, no Department filter is offered here at all (there's only ever one to filter to). Flagged as a deliberate omission vs. the wireframe's Date Range/Department/Vacancy 3-filter row.
- `getManagementDashboard` rewritten: dropped the hardcoded department text under the title (replaced by the new filter bar), removed the vacancies table (moved to the new Department Vacancies page), changed the KPI set from the old ad hoc 5 (Open Vacancies/Applications Received/Interviews Scheduled/Hires/Avg Time to Hire) to the wireframe's 4 (Open Vacancies/Active Candidates/Hires This Month/Rejected), and added a real itemized `attentionItems` list (previously didn't exist at all on this screen) built from three department-wide, not-personal-to-the-user sources: candidates ready for an HM decision (aggregate count, not itemized by name -- Management oversees, doesn't act, matching the wireframe's generic "3 candidates await a decision" vs. the HM dashboard's itemized-by-name version), interviewers with feedback overdue (distinct interviewer count), and OPEN vacancies older than `VACANCY_AGE_NUDGE_DAYS = 30` (implementation default).
- New `getDepartmentVacanciesList` (`GET /management/vacancies`): the table that used to live on the dashboard, now its own page with Date Range + Vacancy filters (no Status filter -- wireframe doesn't show one here either).
- New `getCandidateProgress` (`GET /management/candidate-progress`): Stage Summary tiles (same anchor+round aggregation as Recruitment Progress) plus a "Requires Attention" table covering every currently-in-progress (SHORTLISTED, mid-round) application department-wide, not a narrower attention-worthy subset -- avoids a fragile, opinionated pre-filter hiding candidates who are technically fine but would still be useful to see on a page literally called Candidate Progress. Each row computes `daysAtStage` (from the open `ApplicationStageHistory` entry, same fallback-to-appliedAt pattern as HM's `waitingSince`), a `nextAction` ("Collect Feedback" / "Hiring Decision" / "Advance Decision" / "Awaiting Interview" / "Schedule Interview"), and a `status` (Ready/Delayed/In Progress, with `STAGE_DELAY_DAYS = 5` as another implementation-default threshold).
- New `getUpcomingInterviews` (`GET /management/upcoming-interviews`): future `Interview` rows in department vacancies, Date Range interpreted as a look-ahead window (next 7/30 days) rather than a lookback one like every other page's Date Range filter -- upcoming interviews are always in the future, so the same query-param name was kept for frontend consistency but the meaning flips.
- New report PDFs (`GET /management/reports`, `GET /management/reports/:type/pdf`): 4 types (Department Hiring Summary, Candidate Progress Report, Round Status Report, Vacancy Ageing Report), each a real PDFKit document. Extracted the header/title-block/section-heading/table-drawing code that already existed inline in `reports.controller.ts`'s per-vacancy PDF into `backend/src/utils/pdfReport.ts` as reusable functions -- deliberately did NOT refactor the existing `getVacancyReportPdf` to use them too, since that PDF already works and has been through a real user verification pass; the new util is additive, not a risk to what already shipped. "Round Status Report" required a new department-wide-by-round-order aggregation (`buildRoundStatusData`, grouping `ApplicationStageHistory` rows by round *order* across vacancies rather than by `vacancyStageId`, same reasoning as `aggregateByRoundOrder` elsewhere -- round names/ids aren't comparable across vacancies).

**Frontend:**
- `MgmtLayout.tsx`: sidebar grew from 1 tab to 5 (Dashboard/Department Vacancies/Candidate Progress/Upcoming Interviews/Reports). Also fixed "Altrium HR" -> "Altrium" in the sidebar brand while touching this file (same fix already applied to Interviewer's layout; Leadership's is still pending its own pass).
- New pages: `DepartmentVacanciesPage.tsx`, `CandidateProgressPage.tsx`, `UpcomingInterviewsPage.tsx`, `ReportsPage.tsx` -- all following the established table/filter-bar/status-badge CSS conventions from the HR/HM pages (table-layout fixed, right-aligned last column, 90vw page width). Reports page's View Report/Export both fetch the same authenticated PDF blob (`fetchManagementReportPdfUrl`, same pattern as `fetchVacancyReportPdfUrl`) -- View opens it in a new tab, Export triggers a forced download via a temporary anchor element.
- `DashboardPage.tsx` rewritten: added the Date Range + Vacancy filter bar, removed the vacancies table and hardcoded department text, KPI tiles swapped to label-first/big-number-underneath at 44px (same fix as the HM dashboard), Recruitment Progress + Needs Attention laid out as a parallel two-column grid with the progress tiles on flex-wrap+center (so an incomplete last row centers instead of hugging the left edge), and Needs Attention now renders the real itemized list instead of nothing.

**Demo data:** added `backend/scripts/seed-management-demo.ts` (idempotent, `npx tsx scripts/seed-management-demo.ts`) for `management@altrium.test` (Engineering department) -- 2 vacancies covering every state exercised above: decision-ready, missing-panelist-feedback, hired-this-month, rejected, an upcoming interview, and an old-enough-to-trip-the-ageing-nudge vacancy, plus a fresh on-track vacancy with early-stage candidates for bulk.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend after this pass.

## Leadership screens fixes (corrections doc, task #35)

**Same "1 of 5 tabs exists" pattern as Management, found again.** `LeadershipLayout.tsx` had an explicit code comment locking scope to "Recruitment Overview only... the other 4 tabs [Recruitment Progress, Department Performance, Hiring Trends, Export Reports] are Sprint 2." The corrections doc's wireframes show all 5 as current. Used `AskUserQuestion` before building (same standing rule as every other locked-scope reversal this project) -- confirmed build all 4, matching the precedent already set and confirmed for Management.

**Two wireframe ambiguities resolved via `AskUserQuestion` rather than a silent judgment call**, since guessing wrong here would have meant building the wrong page from scratch:
1. The "Recruitment Progress" tab's wireframe mockup shows content that's actually a report-style table (column layout matching the Reports section, not a progress/stage view), while the tab label and its position in the nav strongly imply it should mirror Management's Candidate Progress page but org-wide. Confirmed with the user: build it as the org-wide equivalent of Candidate Progress (Stage Summary tiles + a "Requires Attention" table), not a literal copy of the mismatched mockup content.
2. The doc has two conflicting drafts of Export Reports: one implies scheduled/recurring report delivery, the other on-demand generation matching Management's Reports page pattern. Confirmed with the user: on-demand, reusing Management's View Report/Export pattern verbatim against Leadership's own report types.

**Department-scoping asymmetry vs. Management, by design, not an oversight.** Leadership is org-wide (`leadership@altrium.test` has `department: null`) rather than locked to one department -- every endpoint accepts an optional `department` filter and every page offers a real Department dropdown (populated via a new `listAllDepartments()` helper, cascading into the Vacancy dropdown's options same as HM/Management's cascading filters), unlike Management which has no Department filter anywhere since there's only ever one department to filter to.

**Backend (all new, `backend/src/controllers/leadership.controller.ts` + `backend/src/routes/leadership.routes.ts`, mounted at `/api/leadership`):**
- `getLeadershipDashboard` (org-wide equivalent of Management's dashboard): same 4 KPIs (Open Vacancies/Active Candidates/Hires This Month/Rejected) plus Recruitment Progress tiles and an itemized `attentionItems` list, but aggregated across every department instead of one, with `department`/`vacancyId` as optional narrowing filters instead of a hard lock. Reused `VACANCY_AGE_NUDGE_DAYS = 30` and `STAGE_DELAY_DAYS = 5` verbatim from Management rather than re-deriving new thresholds -- these are implementation defaults, not per-role tuned values, so there's no reason for Leadership's "ageing vacancy" or "delayed stage" definition to differ from Management's.
- `listLeadershipDepartments`/`listLeadershipVacancies`: new helpers with no Management equivalent, needed specifically because Leadership's filter bar has a real Department dimension that Management's doesn't.
- `getRecruitmentProgress` (`GET /leadership/recruitment-progress`): the org-wide Candidate Progress equivalent confirmed via the AskUserQuestion above -- same Stage Summary + Requires Attention shape as Management's `getCandidateProgress`, with one addition: each attention row includes `vacancy.department` and the frontend table adds a Department column, since an org-wide "requires attention" list is meaningless without saying which department each row belongs to.
- `getDepartmentPerformance` (`GET /leadership/department-performance`, no Management equivalent -- this is Leadership-only cross-department comparison): groups vacancies and hired/rejected applications by department, computing `fillRate` (closed/total vacancies as a percentage), `avgTimeToHireDays` (from hired applications' `decidedAt - appliedAt`), and a `summary` block (`bestFillRate`/`fastestHiring`/`mostOpenRoles`/`overdueRoles`) for the page's headline KPI tiles, plus sorted `hiredByDepartment`/`rejectedByDepartment` arrays for the bar-chart panels and a full `rows` table for the department-by-department breakdown.
- `getHiringTrends` (`GET /leadership/hiring-trends`, no Management equivalent): applications/candidatesInRounds/hired/rejected counts plus an 8-month (`TREND_MONTHS = 8`) hires-per-month trend keyed by `decidedAt`'s calendar month, for the page's line chart.
- Report PDFs (`GET /leadership/reports`, `GET /leadership/reports/:type/pdf`): 4 org-wide types (Recruitment Performance, Department Performance, Round Performance, Vacancy Status) mirroring Management's 4-report structure and reusing the same `backend/src/utils/pdfReport.ts` drawing primitives, badged "LEADERSHIP REPORT" instead of "MANAGEMENT REPORT".
- Wrote filter types with explicit `| undefined` (`{ dateRange?: DateRangeFilter | undefined; department?: string | undefined; vacancyId?: number | undefined }`) from the first draft, avoiding the `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` errors that had to be fixed after the fact in `management.controller.ts`.

**Frontend:**
- `LeadershipLayout.tsx`: nav grew from 1 tab to 5 (Recruitment Overview/Recruitment Progress/Department Performance/Hiring Trends/Export Reports); "Altrium HR" -> "Altrium" brand fix applied (same fix already done on Interviewer's and Management's layouts).
- `RecruitmentOverviewPage.tsx` (existing page, reworked): added the cascading Date Range/Department/Vacancy filter bar, KPI tiles swapped to label-first/44px-number (same convention as HM/Management dashboards), Recruitment Progress + Needs Attention laid out as the same parallel two-column grid with flex-wrap+center progress tiles.
- New `RecruitmentProgressPage.tsx`: Stage Summary tile grid + Requires Attention table with the extra Department column noted above.
- New `DepartmentPerformancePage.tsx`: 4 summary KPI tiles (Best Fill Rate/Fastest Hiring/Most Open Roles/Overdue Roles), two side-by-side horizontal bar-chart panels (Hired by Department, Rejected by Department) following the existing `.cc-bar-track`/`.cc-bar-fill` visual pattern from `CandidateComparisonPage.css` since no charting library is installed in the frontend, plus a full per-department data table below the charts.
- New `HiringTrendsPage.tsx`: 4 KPI tiles plus a hand-built inline SVG line chart (no library -- same reasoning as the bar charts) plotting the 8-month hire trend, with per-point value labels and month labels on the x-axis.
- New `ExportReportsPage.tsx`: direct structural reuse of Management's `ReportsPage.tsx` (View Report opens a blob URL in a new tab, Export forces a download via a temporary anchor), pointed at `leadership.ts`'s API functions and its own 4 report types -- Leadership has no `hasDepartment` gate unlike Management's page, since Leadership always has data to scope (org-wide, never blocked on a missing department).
- All 4 new pages, plus the reworked Dashboard, follow the established `max-width: 90vw` page-width convention and the flex-wrap+justify-content:center "self-centering incomplete row" pattern for tile grids.
- `App.tsx`: added imports + 4 new routes under `/leadership-management/` (`recruitment-progress`, `department-performance`, `hiring-trends`, `reports`), alongside the existing `recruitment-overview` route.

**Demo data:** added `backend/scripts/seed-leadership-demo.ts` (idempotent, `npx tsx scripts/seed-leadership-demo.ts`) for `leadership@altrium.test` (org-wide, no department lock) -- unlike Management's single-department seed, this one deliberately spans 3 departments (Engineering, Sales, Marketing) so Department Performance's cross-department comparison has real variation to show: Engineering gets the full state spread (decision-ready, missing-feedback, hired, rejected, upcoming interview, ageing vacancy) from a copy of Management's demo scenario; Sales gets one on-track vacancy with a hire and a rejection as a second comparison point; Marketing gets one overdue vacancy with no hires yet, so it surfaces as the worst-performing department on fill rate.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend after this pass. Not executed against the real database from this session -- same standing sandbox limitation as every other seed script this project (the dev MySQL instance is only reachable from the user's own machine); the user runs `npx tsx scripts/seed-leadership-demo.ts` from the `backend` folder themselves.

**IT Admin (task #36) is the only role left with an outstanding corrections-doc pass.** All 5 roles' screens (HR, Interviewer, Hiring Manager, Management, Leadership) are now built out and verified compiling; only IT Admin's fixes (Deactivate-button relocation, Create User becoming a full redirect page, Audit Logs column/label changes, a new Systems page) remain from the original corrections-doc audit.

## IT Admin screens fixes (corrections doc, task #36)

**New schema field, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed.** The Create User wireframe shows a "Contact Number" field, but `User.phoneNumber` doesn't exist -- an earlier explicit decision in this log ("Schema — Candidate Phone Number" section) said staff don't need phone numbers, only candidates do. Rather than silently reopening that decision or silently dropping the wireframe's field, asked directly: confirmed adding a real nullable `User.phoneNumber` column. Migration `20260831103000_add_user_phone_number` was hand-written (same `ALTER TABLE` shape as the earlier `add_candidate_phone_number` migration) since the sandbox has no DB access to run `prisma migrate dev` itself -- **the user needs to run `npx prisma migrate dev` (or apply the migration and run `npx prisma generate`) on their own machine before Create User's Contact Number field will actually persist; until then the stale generated client will throw "Unknown argument phoneNumber" if that field is filled in.**

**Second `AskUserQuestion` on the new Systems page**, since there's no monitoring or backup infrastructure anywhere in this project and the wireframe implies both exist. Confirmed: real, honestly-derived values rather than fabricated ones, and backups reported as "not yet configured" rather than a fake "successful" line.

**Backend:**
- New `POST /api/auth/verify-password` (`auth.controller.ts`, any authenticated role): re-confirms the caller's *own* password mid-session (bcrypt-compares against their own `passwordHash`, no new token issued). This is the one new piece of shared infrastructure both password-confirm flows below needed -- didn't exist anywhere before since nothing previously asked a logged-in user to re-prove their identity mid-session.
- `createUser` (`user.controller.ts`) now accepts an optional `phoneNumber` and passes it through.
- `writeAuditLog` call sites extended with the target's **name** (not just email) so Audit Logs descriptions don't need extra lookups at read time: `ACCOUNT_CREATED`/`ACCOUNT_DEACTIVATED`/`ROLE_CHANGED` (`user.controller.ts`) gained `name`; `CV_UPLOADED` (`candidate.controller.ts`) gained `name`; `FEEDBACK_SUBMITTED` (`feedback.controller.ts`) gained `candidateName`, which required widening the interview lookup's `select` to include `application.candidate.name`.
- `auditLog.controller.ts` rewritten: new `EVENT_TYPE` map groups the 11 `AuditAction` values into the wireframe's categories (Vacancy Management, Candidate Management, Record Feedback, Interview Management, Notifications, User Management, Hiring Decisions); new `describeAction()` builds the specific sentence the wireframe asks for ("Submitted feedback for Amanda Silva") from each action's metadata instead of a generic category label, with graceful fallbacks for older rows missing a field. `listAuditLogs` now filters by `eventType`/`from`/`to` (dropped the old raw `action`/`entityType` filters) and returns `{id, createdAt, user:{id,name}, eventType, description}` per row -- `entityType`/`entityId`/raw `metadata` are no longer sent to the frontend at all, structurally enforcing "no Entity/Details columns" rather than just hiding them client-side. New `GET /api/audit-logs/event-types` feeds the filter dropdown. New `system.controller.ts`/`system.routes.ts` (`GET /api/system/metrics`, IT_ADMIN only).
- New `backend/src/utils/systemMetrics.ts`: `requestTimingMiddleware` (wired into `app.ts` ahead of all routers) keeps a rolling window of the last 200 request durations for a real "Response time" average; `getServerLoadPercent()` reads `os.loadavg()` as a percentage of CPU count -- flagged in a code comment and to the user that this always reads 0% on Windows-hosted dev servers (a Node/libuv limitation, not something the app can work around) since `os.loadavg()` only returns real values on Linux/macOS. "Concurrent users" has no session table to count (stateless JWT auth) -- used the closest honest proxy instead: distinct `AuditLog.userId` values in the last 5 minutes, documented in a code comment as a proxy, not a literal connection count.

**Frontend:**
- New shared `components/PasswordConfirmModal.tsx`: a generic "type your password to confirm" modal (verifies via the new endpoint, then runs the caller's action, surfacing any failure -- wrong password or the underlying action's own error -- in the same modal). Used in three places: Users page's deactivate/activate action, Users page's pre-navigation gate on Create User, and Create User page's final submit.
- `UsersPage.tsx`: removed the standalone per-row Deactivate/Activate button entirely. The edit-pencil popup gained a read-only "Created: ..." line (informational, not an editable field, per the doc) and a Deactivate/Activate button in its footer that routes through `PasswordConfirmModal` before calling `setUserActive`, then shows a dismissable success banner ("X was successfully deactivated/activated") at the top of the page. The old inline Create User modal was deleted outright; the Create button now opens `PasswordConfirmModal` first and only navigates to `/admin/users/create` once that resolves. Page widened from a fixed `1100px` to `90vw` to match the "reach the corner" convention used everywhere else this session (not explicitly called out for this page by name, but the doc's general "space and align the content properly" note applies the same way it did elsewhere).
- New `pages/admin/CreateUserPage.tsx`/`.css` (`/admin/users/create`): breadcrumb "Users / Create User", matches the wireframe's Name/Contact Number/Email/Role fields plus a still-necessary Initial Password field the wireframe didn't show (the account is created immediately, there's no email-invite flow, so a password has to come from somewhere -- kept the existing modal's precedent rather than inventing a different mechanism). Submitting routes through `PasswordConfirmModal` a second time before actually calling `createUser`, then swaps to a success view ("User Created" + Back to Users / Create Another) instead of a toast, matching "and then success" being shown as its own page state.
- `auditLogs.ts`/`AuditLogsPage.tsx` rewritten around the new backend shape: filter bar is now From/To/Event Type + Apply (dropped the old All-actions dropdown, free-text entity-type input, and Clear button), using the same staged-filter-committed-on-Apply pattern as every other filter bar this session. Table columns are now Timestamp/User/Action/Event Type -- Time renamed to Timestamp, User shows only the name (the backend response no longer even includes email), Action renders as plain text via `description` instead of a blue pill badge, Entity and Details columns are gone (there's no data left to show since the backend stopped sending them). Page widened to `90vw`.
- New `pages/admin/SystemPage.tsx`/`.css` (`/admin/system`, replacing the `ComingSoon` placeholder -- which was then deleted from `App.tsx` since nothing else used it): a Metric/Value table (Server load, Response time, Concurrent users) plus a Backups section, with a small note explaining the Windows load-average caveat and what "concurrent users" actually measures, so the numbers don't read as more authoritative than they are.
- `App.tsx`: added `CreateUserPage`/`SystemPage` imports and their two routes; `ITAdminLayout.tsx`'s sidebar brand was already "Altrium IT Admin" (matches the wireframe exactly), so unlike every other role's layout this one needed no brand-text fix.

**Not built, flagged rather than silently skipped:** real automated database backups. The Systems page's Backups section honestly reports "not yet configured" -- building an actual scheduled `mysqldump` job (storage location, retention, failure alerting) is materially new infrastructure work, not a frontend corrections-doc fix, and was explicitly scoped out via the `AskUserQuestion` above.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend after this pass. Not verified end-to-end against the real database (same standing sandbox limitation as every other schema/data change this project) -- the user needs to run the new migration (see above) before Create User's Contact Number field will work, and the seed/regression scripts don't yet exercise any IT Admin screen specifically (no `seed-itadmin-demo.ts` was requested or built this pass; the existing seeded `itadmin@altrium.test` account plus whatever accounts already exist in the database are enough to exercise Users/Audit Logs/System as built).

### Follow-up round, from live screenshots (2026-08-31)

**IT Admin login page:** removed the "This entry point is for IT Admin accounts only." notice block from `AdminLogin.tsx` entirely (`login-notice` div deleted). Also fixed a real gap: `.login-password-wrap`/`.login-toggle` were referenced in `AdminLogin.tsx`'s JSX but never actually defined in `Login.css` -- the Show/Hide button was rendering as an unstyled default HTML button. Added proper styling (gold-accent text button positioned inside the password field, matching the site's pill/gold-outline aesthetic) to `Login.css` so it now matches the rest of the app instead of looking like a browser default.

**Create User page, made more resilient rather than just re-explained.** After the first explanation of the pending-migration issue, the user tried again with realistic data (still pre-migration) and hit the same generic "Could not create user" failure -- confirming the failure mode was blocking real use, not just a one-off. Rather than asking a third time whether the migration had been run, made `createUser` (`user.controller.ts`) resilient to the exact failure: it now tries the insert with `phoneNumber` first, and if Prisma throws a validation error for the unknown argument (checked via `err.name === "PrismaClientValidationError"` or the error message containing "Unknown argument" -- a narrow check so it doesn't accidentally swallow unrelated validation failures), it retries once without `phoneNumber` and returns `phoneNumberSaved: false` in the response. The frontend (`CreateUserPage.tsx`) surfaces that as an inline warning on the success screen ("Note: the Contact Number wasn't saved...") instead of failing the whole account creation over one optional field. This means IT Admin can create accounts today, before the migration is run -- Contact Number just won't persist until it is. Also widened the form to fill the full page width (removed a `900px` cap, `.cru-form-box` is now `width:100%` inside the already-90vw page) with larger field padding/gaps, since the form was leaving the whole right half of the screen empty and only using the top portion vertically.

**Systems page Backups section — explicit reversal of the earlier "show it honestly as not configured" decision.** The original `AskUserQuestion` (this same session, above) was answered "real, derived values... backups reported as not yet configured." Shown the result, the user explicitly asked to see it look like a working system instead, including a backup history, not a labeled "preview." This is a direct, unambiguous override of that earlier answer, not a case for asking again -- complied, but documenting the reversal here per the standing rule that decisions get updated in place with the new reasoning, not silently replaced.

Implementation: `system.controller.ts`'s `getSystemMetrics` now returns `backups: { status: "successful", lastBackupAt, history: [...] }` where `history` is built by `buildSimulatedBackupHistory()` -- a deterministic, clearly-commented function that fabricates one "Successful" entry per day for the last 7 days at 03:00 UTC. This is explicitly a display-only simulation: there is still no scheduler, no `mysqldump`, no backup storage anywhere in this project. The code comment and this log entry exist specifically so this isn't later mistaken for a real backup log by future-me or anyone reading the code. Frontend (`SystemPage.tsx`) dropped the gold "Preview" tag/caption entirely and renders the status banner (now green/"Successful" styled) plus a real-looking Date/Status history table, indistinguishable in the UI from a genuine backup log.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend after this round.

**Create User success screen redesign + removal of phoneNumberWarning note.** User feedback on the success screen: center it, make it visually signal something important happened, and remove the "Note: the Contact Number wasn't saved..." migration-pending warning entirely. Implemented in `CreateUserPage.tsx`/`.css`: success state now renders a centered card (`.cru-success-wrap` flex-centers `.cru-success-box`) with a green top-accent border, soft shadow, and a green circular checkmark icon (`.cru-success-icon`) above the title, replacing the plain left-aligned block. The `phoneNumberWarning` state, its conditional paragraph, and the `.cru-warning` CSS block were deleted outright per explicit instruction -- the UI no longer discloses whether the Contact Number was actually persisted. Note this is a UX-only change: `createUser`'s retry-without-phoneNumber fallback (previous entry) is unchanged, so accounts created before the migration is run still silently drop the Contact Number; that is simply no longer surfaced to the IT Admin in the UI. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on frontend after this change.

**Sidebar pinned across all 6 role layouts — Log out no longer scrolls off-screen.** User feedback (from the IT Admin Users page, which has enough rows to scroll): the sidebar was scrolling along with the main content, so on long pages the Log out button could end up off-screen below the fold. Root cause: every layout (`ITAdminLayout`, `LeadershipLayout`, `MgmtLayout`, `InterviewerLayout`, `HMLayout`, `HRLayout`) used `min-height: 100vh` on the outer flex row with no independent scroll region, so a tall `<main>` stretched the whole row (sidebar included) past the viewport and the browser scrolled the entire page together. Fixed identically across all 6: the outer `.xx-layout` is now `height: 100vh; overflow: hidden` (a hard cap, not a minimum), the `.xx-sidebar` gets `height: 100%; overflow-y: auto` so it's always exactly viewport-tall (Log out stays pinned at the bottom via the existing `.xx-nav { flex: 1 }` spacer), and `.xx-main` gets `height: 100%; overflow-y: auto` so only the content area scrolls, independent of the sidebar. HR's `.hr-main` kept its existing `overflow-x: auto`/`min-width: 0` (needed for wide tables) alongside the new `overflow-y: auto`. `RoleDashboard.tsx` (a generic unstyled placeholder, not part of the 6 real role layouts) was left untouched. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on frontend.

Built a reusable `components/Toast.tsx`/`.css` (bottom-center, fixed position, slide-up-and-fade-in animation, green success / red error variants matching the existing success-screen palette, auto-calls `onClose` via `setTimeout` after a `duration` prop defaulting to 7000ms) rather than a one-off fix, since this is a generic pattern (any page with a transient confirmation can reuse it). **Not run against the real database from this sandbox** -- same standing limitation as every other seed script in this project (dev MySQL is only reachable from the user's own machine). To get a clean slate, the user needs to run, from the `backend` folder, in this order: `npx tsx scripts/wipe-candidates.ts` (already existed -- wipes every candidate and vacancy, including the old demo data and any stray hand-created vacancies/candidates, and leaves user accounts untouched); then `npx tsx scripts/delete-staff.ts "<email>"` once per old demo panelist account to remove the leftover Users-page rows those old scripts created (`priya.panelist.hm-demo@example.com`, `ravi.panelist.hm-demo@example.com`, `dana.panelist.ld-demo@example.com`, `eli.panelist.ld-demo@example.com`, `fay.panelist.ld-demo@example.com`, `dana.panelist.mgmt-demo@example.com`, `eli.panelist.mgmt-demo@example.com`); then the 4 new seed scripts (`seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts`, `seed-leadership-screens.ts`, `seed-management-screens.ts`, `seed-followups-screen.ts`).

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend (new scripts type-check) and frontend (branding changes) after this round.

**Dark-mode logo glitch: added `<meta name="color-scheme" content="light">`.** User reported the AltriumLogo SVG (a plain hardcoded-color hexagon, no dark-mode-aware CSS at all) rendering with a colored fringe along its edges when their OS/browser dark mode was on. `index.css` already had `color-scheme: light` on `:root`, but that's a CSS-time signal; browsers' automatic "force dark mode for pages that don't support it" heuristic can act before/independently of page CSS. Added the same signal as an HTML `<meta>` tag in `frontend/index.html`'s `<head>`, which is the standard, earlier-available way to tell the browser this page is light-only and skip the forced-dark color-inversion pass that was producing the artifact on the SVG's solid-fill polygon edges. Also fixed `index.html`'s `<title>`, which was still the Vite default "frontend" -- now "Altrium". Root cause is a browser/OS feature, not application code, so if this persists after the meta tag it's worth checking whether a "Force dark mode" browser setting or extension is enabled for this site specifically.

**CVs: 37 real, well-formatted PDF resumes generated and wired into the seed scripts.** User asked for real PDF CVs, not the previous placeholder string (`cv-on-file.pdf`, a filename with no file behind it -- "CV file not found in storage" was the correct, honest error for every seeded candidate before this). Generated one realistic one-page PDF resume per candidate across all 4 seed scripts (37 total: name/contact header, professional summary, two experience entries with role-appropriate bullet points, education, and a skills list -- content varies by the vacancy's role title, e.g. backend engineer candidates get Node.js/TypeScript/PostgreSQL, product designer candidates get Figma/User Research/Design Systems) via a Python/reportlab script, saved into `backend/uploads/cvs/` following the same `<slug>_cv.pdf` naming the app itself would generate for a real upload. Each seed script's `ensureCandidate()` now sets `cvUrl` to that real filename via a matching `slugify()` helper instead of the old shared placeholder string, so every seeded candidate's CV actually opens.

While placing these, found and deleted 23 pre-existing orphaned files already sitting in `backend/uploads/cvs/` from earlier manual testing -- several literally named `14_Regression_Candidate_...pdf` / `17_Regression_Candidate_B_...pdf`, the rest UUID-named leftovers from candidates that had since been deleted. None were referenced by any current candidate (the user's own `wipe-candidates.ts` run just before this had already cleared every candidate row), so removing them was a clean no-op for the running app and finishes the "no test/demo/regression anywhere" request at the filesystem level, not just in the database.

**Correction to prior instructions: `delete-staff.ts` usage.** The previous message's cleanup instructions showed `npx tsx scripts/delete-staff.ts "<email>"` as a template the user was meant to substitute -- they ran it literally with the placeholder text still in it, which predictably matched nothing. Real emails needed (the 7 old demo-panelist accounts, still present since `wipe-candidates.ts` only touches candidates/vacancies, not users): `priya.panelist.hm-demo@example.com`, `ravi.panelist.hm-demo@example.com`, `dana.panelist.ld-demo@example.com`, `eli.panelist.ld-demo@example.com`, `fay.panelist.ld-demo@example.com`, `dana.panelist.mgmt-demo@example.com`, `eli.panelist.mgmt-demo@example.com`.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend (new cvUrl/slugify code in all 4 scripts) and frontend (index.html change doesn't affect the build) after this round.

**Report PDF export bug: fixed the interleaved header/async-fetch pattern in `management.controller.ts` and `leadership.controller.ts`.** User reported a corrupted/failed download (`ERR_FAILED`) when exporting the Management "Hiring Summary" report. Root cause: `getReportPdf` in both controllers set `Content-Type`/`Content-Disposition` and called `doc.pipe(res)` *before* completing the async data-fetch (`buildDashboardData`, etc.) and before/during PDFKit's drawing calls. Any error thrown during that fetch or draw tried to fall back to `res.status(500).json(...)` after PDF bytes had already started streaming -- which can't produce a valid JSON response on top of a partially-sent binary stream, corrupting the download. Fixed in both files by splitting into two phases: an initial try/catch that awaits all the data first and returns a clean `res.status(500).json(...)` on failure (no headers sent yet), followed by a second try/catch that sets headers, builds and streams the PDF using the already-fetched data, and on failure just logs and calls `res.end()` (since headers may already be sent by then, a second JSON response isn't possible). Checked `candidate.controller.ts` and `reports.controller.ts` for the same pattern (both also serve PDFs) -- both already fetch their data fully before calling `res.setHeader`, so they don't have this bug and weren't touched. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend.

**HR Vacancies department grid: added vacancies to all 8 canonical departments.** The HR Vacancies page's department-grid view uses its own hardcoded 8-department list (`HR`, `Finance and Accounting`, `Operations`, `Marketing`, `Sales`, `IT`, `Customer Service`, `Legal`) -- separate from the freeform department strings (`Engineering`, `Data`, `Design`, `Support`, etc.) used by Management/HM/Leadership screens. Before this, only Marketing, Sales, and Customer Service had any vacancy at all (one each, incidentally created by other seed scripts), so the other 5 department cards showed "No vacancies in this department yet." New script `seed-hr-department-vacancies.ts` adds 2 open vacancies to each of the 5 empty departments and 1 more each to Marketing/Sales/Customer Service, all with realistic titles/descriptions and a 2-stage pipeline (Screening + Final Interview), safe to re-run.

**Interview time display: added a 12-hour start-end range instead of a single raw timestamp.** User feedback: the day-panel in `InterviewsPage.tsx` showed a bare `12:07`-style time with no explicit AM/PM and no end time (traced to `toLocaleTimeString` with no `hour12` flag, silently falling back to the browser/OS locale's 24-hour default). Added a `formatSlotTimeRange` helper that formats the slot's start time as `h:mm AM/PM` and computes an implicit 1-hour end time (there's no separate duration field on `InterviewSlot`, so a fixed 60-minute block is treated as the default, matching how slots are scheduled elsewhere) -- e.g. "9:00 AM - 10:00 AM". Two other raw time displays in the same file (the slot-detail panel header, and the "Add to Interview" modal's dropdown options) were deliberately left as-is since they weren't part of what the user pointed at.

**Interview dates consolidated onto one shared busy past day, with a flagged tradeoff.** User asked to remove all the scattered interview dates and leave only one date ("19") highlighted on the calendar, with multiple candidates showing when clicked. Root cause of the scatter: each seed script's `ensureInterview(offsetDays)` helper computed `scheduledAt` from `Date.now() + offsetDays * DAY_MS` with no explicit time-of-day, so different candidates using different day offsets (needed to represent different points in their own interview timeline) each landed on their own separate day, and even candidates sharing an offset landed within seconds of each other rather than at distinct times. Fix: every "already happened" interview across all 4 seed scripts (`seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts`, `seed-leadership-screens.ts`, `seed-management-screens.ts`, `seed-followups-screen.ts`) now uses a shared `pastDay` constant (12 days before whenever the script is run) plus an explicit staggered time via a new `ensureInterviewAt`/`atTime` helper pair (added to the 3 scripts that didn't already have one), instead of each candidate's own offset. That's 16 interviews now landing on one calendar date instead of 8+ separate ones.

Flagging a real constraint rather than silently picking a side: a handful of interviews are genuinely meant to be *upcoming*, not historical -- Ben Castillo ("mid-round, not interviewed yet") and Olga Petrova / Elise Fontaine (drive the Upcoming Interviews widgets on HM/Management/Leadership dashboards) and Mateo Reyes (drives Follow-Ups' "Calls" section) all still use forward offsets, and the pre-existing "busy Thursday" block (Grace/Marcus/Sofia/Tomasz) is also intentionally future-dated. Removing those would break the very dashboard states they exist to demo. Left them untouched -- since they all use the same `+3`-day-ish offsets/`nextWeekday(Thursday)` logic, they land on the same future date as each other when the scripts are run on the same day, so the practical result is two highlighted calendar dates (one big past day, one smaller future day) rather than strictly one, but no longer the many scattered single-candidate days from before.

**Candidate Comparison: added scored candidates to "Support Specialist" so it isn't empty.** User reported the Comparison page showing nothing when "Support Specialist" was selected. Cause: every existing candidate on that vacancy (Hank Sullivan, Ivy Chen) already has a final `hiringDecision` (HIRED/REJECTED), and Comparison only has something to rank while a decision is still pending -- there were no still-in-progress, scored candidates left. Added 3 new SHORTLISTED candidates (Jasper Wells, Keisha Bryant, Leo Marchetti) with panel feedback scores, following the same pattern already working for "Product Designer".

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend after this round (all edits are backend controllers + seed scripts).

## Real Email Delivery + Email History (tasks #43/#44)

**Real SMTP email delivery wired via nodemailer, with automatic dev fallback.** `backend/src/utils/mailer.ts` was a permanent console-log stub. Since real delivery needs an actual mail account, the user chose: wire nodemailer to read connection details from environment variables, rather than either leaving it stubbed forever or having me fabricate/request credentials that couldn't be tested from this sandbox anyway (no route to an external SMTP provider without the user present). `sendEmail()` now checks whether `SMTP_HOST`/`SMTP_USER`/`SMTP_PASS` are set: if not, it falls back to the original console-log behavior unchanged (so teammates without a configured mail account see no difference); if set, it sends via a lazily-created nodemailer transport (`SMTP_HOST`, `SMTP_PORT` default 587, `SMTP_SECURE` default false/STARTTLS, `SMTP_USER`/`SMTP_PASS`, `MAIL_FROM` falling back to `SMTP_USER`). Added `nodemailer`/`@types/nodemailer` to `backend/package.json`. Added the 5 new keys (blank) to the real `.env` and created `backend/.env.example` documenting both an Outlook/Microsoft 365 and a Gmail setup (Gmail requires an App Password specifically, not the account's normal login password).

Fixed a latent bug this change exposed: `auth.controller.ts`'s forgot-password handler called `sendEmail()` unguarded, whereas every other call site already wraps it in try/catch. The old stub could never throw, so this was invisible; a real SMTP failure would have thrown past the handler's "always respond identically whether or not the account exists" design (the comment directly above explains why -- a differing response would leak account existence), producing a 500 instead of the generic message on a random subset of requests. Wrapped it in try/catch to match the security property the code already documented but didn't actually enforce.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend.

**Email History added to the Candidate Detail page.** Read-only list (not a sidebar tab, per the task's explicit note) showing every email sent to this candidate -- e.g. "Interview invitation - Sent - 30 Aug 2026." Backend: `getCandidate` in `candidate.controller.ts` now also queries `AuditLog` for `NOTIFICATION_SENT` entries and filters in JS for `metadata.recipient` matching the candidate's email (JS-side filtering chosen over a JSON-path Prisma query since `metadata` is a generic `Json` column shared by 10 other unrelated `AuditAction` shapes -- a JSON-path filter would only be simpler for this one caller and riskier across the others), mapping each match's `reason` to a human label via a small `CANDIDATE_EMAIL_LABEL` table (`hiring_decision_hire` -> "Offer email", `hiring_decision_reject` -> "Rejection email", `interview_scheduled_candidate` -> "Interview invitation", anything else falls back to a generic "Email" rather than disappearing). As flagged when this task was deferred: `AuditLog.metadata` only ever stored `{recipient, channel, reason}`, never the actual subject/body sent, so this shows "what kind of email, when" only -- not full message content -- unless the send paths are separately updated to persist that. Frontend: added `emailHistory` to the `CandidateDetail` type in `api/candidates.ts` and a new "Email History" card on `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`, placed between Applicant History and Hiring Manager, reusing the existing `.cnd-review-list`/`.cnd-summary-row` styling already used by those two sections rather than introducing new CSS.

Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend.

## "Is everything done?" audit + automated test suite (unit/logic only)

User asked whether all of Sprint 1 and Sprint 2's coding was actually finished, and if so, to move to automated testing covering every scenario -- explicitly conditional on "only if everything is done." Verified rather than assumed: all 82 tracked tasks were complete, `tsc --noEmit` was clean on both sides, the migrations folder had 17 entries ending in one dated the same morning, and a sweep of this log for "not built"/"still pending"/"flagged but not fixed" turned up nothing outstanding except two things -- the feedback score validation gap (already closed, confirmed by reading `feedback.controller.ts` directly) and the stale `User.phoneNumber` line corrected above. Reported this to the user with the caveat that the log itself isn't a fully reliable "what's built" source right now.

**Test scope decision:** this project had zero test infrastructure (no Jest/Vitest/Playwright in either `package.json`). Since this sandbox has no access to the real MySQL database or a live browser session against the app, true integration/E2E testing can only be written here, not executed or verified here. Presented three options; user chose unit/logic tests only -- mocking Prisma and fetch, covering business logic without needing the real DB, fully runnable and verifiable from this sandbox.

**Backend:** added `vitest` + `vitest-mock-extended` as dev dependencies, `backend/vitest.config.ts` (node environment), and `"test": "vitest run"` in `package.json`. 6 test files, 43 tests, all passing:
- `feedback.test.ts` -- `isValidScore` range/type edge cases (exported the previously-local function for testability).
- `auditLog.test.ts` -- `EVENT_TYPE` categorization and `describeAction`'s per-action sentence generation, including the "missing metadata field" fallback paths (both exported for testability).
- `reportPdf.test.ts` -- the highest-value test in the suite: a direct regression test for this session's PDF-export bug fix in both `management.controller.ts` and `leadership.controller.ts`. Mocks `../prisma.js` with an auto-mock Proxy where every Prisma call resolves to `undefined` (deliberately -- `buildDashboardData`/etc. always expect a real array back, so touching `.length`/`.map` on `undefined` throws naturally, exercising the failure path without needing realistic seeded data for 8 different report types), then asserts `res.setHeader` is never called and the client gets a clean `{error: "Could not build report PDF"}` JSON 500 -- exactly the contract the fetch-then-stream split is supposed to guarantee. Also covers invalid report type and missing-department 400s.
- `cvExtraction.test.ts` -- `guessName`'s header/email/phone/too-long/first-8-lines heuristics, plus `EMAIL_REGEX`/`PHONE_REGEX` (all exported for testability).
- `fileStorage.test.ts` -- `sanitizeForFilename`'s character-stripping, truncation, and empty-input fallback.
- `mailer.test.ts` -- both branches of this session's new `sendEmail()`: falls back to console logging when SMTP env vars are unset or only partially set, sends via a mocked `nodemailer` transport when fully configured (asserting the exact `createTransport`/`sendMail` args), and confirms the transporter is cached across multiple sends rather than reconnecting every time. Uses `vi.doMock` + `vi.resetModules()` per test so the module-level `cachedTransporter` never leaks between tests.

Every controller-touching test file mocks `"../prisma.js"` with a self-contained Proxy-based auto-mock (any `prisma.<model>.<method>(...)` call resolves to `undefined`) rather than `vitest-mock-extended`'s `mockDeep` -- simpler to get right given `vi.mock` factories can't safely reference outer-scope variables without extra hoisting ceremony, and "resolves to undefined" is exactly the shape needed to provoke a natural downstream throw for the failure-path tests. This mock is required even for pure-function tests (e.g. `isValidScore`) because `src/prisma.ts` constructs a real `PrismaMariaDb` adapter from `process.env.DATABASE_URL` at module scope, which crashes immediately in a sandbox with no DB connection and no `.env` loading in the test runner.

**Frontend:** the original plan (Vitest + jsdom + React Testing Library) hit a hard environment wall: this sandbox's Linux shell mounts the user's actual Windows project folder, and `npm install` for jsdom/testing-library repeatedly either timed out (single shell calls appear capped around ~170s regardless of the timeout requested) or corrupted `node_modules` with `ENOTEMPTY`/`Input/output error` failures on `@napi-rs`/`@rolldown` native binding folders -- classic cross-filesystem (Linux-process-writing-to-an-NTFS-mount) rename/lock semantics mismatch, not a code problem. Descoped to pure-logic tests only (matches the user's chosen scope anyway) and extracted the two testable helpers out of their page components into standalone, dependency-free modules so they need nothing beyond Vitest itself: `formatSlotTimeRange` moved from `InterviewsPage.tsx` into `src/utils/interviewTime.ts`, and `stageDisplayFor` moved from `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` into `src/utils/candidateStage.ts` (both pages updated to import from the new location; behavior unchanged). Added `vitest` as a dev dependency, a minimal `frontend/vitest.config.ts` (deliberately a plain object with zero imports -- importing even `vitest/config` requires local resolution that a partially-broken `node_modules` can't guarantee, and the project's real `vite.config.ts` needs `vite`/`@vitejs/plugin-react`, which unit tests have no reason to load), and `"test": "vitest run --config vitest.config.ts"`. 2 test files, 9 tests, all passing: `interviewTime.test.ts` (AM/PM formatting, noon/midnight rollover, regression-pins the exact "9:00 AM - 10:00 AM" bug fixed earlier this session) and `candidateStage.test.ts` (plain vs. rejected display, and the edge case of a rejection before any interview round shouldn't render "- Rejected" off an empty round name).

**Known fallout, flagged rather than hidden:** the repeated `npm install` attempts while chasing jsdom left the frontend's real `node_modules` (the user's actual Windows folder, not a disposable sandbox copy) in a partially broken state -- confirmed by `node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc` failing with "Cannot find global type 'Array'"/missing `lib.dom.d.ts`, i.e. the `typescript` package itself is incompletely installed. **This means frontend `tsc --noEmit` could not be re-verified from this sandbox after the `InterviewsPage.tsx`/`CandidateDetailPage.tsx` extraction edits** -- they were checked by hand instead (import added/removed cleanly, no leftover references to the old local function definitions, call sites unchanged). The user needs to run `npm install` in `frontend/` on their own machine before their next `npm run dev`/build, and should run `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` once to confirm the extraction compiles cleanly (expected to pass -- it's a pure move, not a logic change). Backend's `node_modules` was not affected by this (only additive `npm install -D` calls, no `rm -rf`) and its `tsc --noEmit` stayed clean throughout.

**Not covered by this suite, by design (unit/logic scope, not integration/E2E):** anything that requires a real database round-trip (readiness gates, stage-transition guards, the full HM decision flow, seed-data-dependent screens), anything requiring a rendered DOM (Toast's auto-dismiss timer, form validation UI), and anything requiring a live server (auth middleware, route wiring, file upload). If the user wants that coverage later, the two natural next steps are: point a real test database at a mocked/throwaway MySQL instance for backend integration tests, or add jsdom/Testing Library once `npm install` is confirmed healthy on the user's own machine for frontend component tests.

## Automated E2E test suite (Selenium) — Sprint 1 + Sprint 2

User asked for a full Selenium suite covering every Sprint 1 and Sprint 2 user story, matching the style taught in lectures (`webdriver.Chrome()`, `WebDriverWait`/`expected_conditions`, `By.ID`/`By.CSS_SELECTOR` locators, PASS/FAIL console output, a `check_servers_are_up()` pre-flight check) rather than the Vitest unit tests above, which only cover pure logic. This is real browser automation against the actual running app — it needs both servers up and a real DB, so unlike the Vitest suite it can only be written and statically verified from this sandbox, not executed end-to-end here (no live browser/DB access). **Done — written, not run.**

Before writing a single script, read or grepped every page component that Sprint 1/2 test cases touch (`Login.tsx`, `AdminLogin.tsx`, all of `hr/`, `hiringManager/`, `management/`, `leadership/`, `interviewer/`, `admin/`, plus `App.tsx` for the exact route table) to pull real `id`/`className` selectors, exact validation error strings, and button labels — the same standard already applied when validating the user's pasted `test_login.py` example. Caught and fixed one real bug during this pass: `frontend/src/api/candidates.ts`'s CV upload review-row inputs have no `id`, so the test targets them by XPath relative to their `<label>` text instead of guessing an id that doesn't exist.

**Structure:** `automated-tests/selenium/` at the project root — `helpers.py` (shared login-by-role, explicit-wait wrappers, PASS/FAIL reporter, and a hand-built minimal-PDF generator for CV-upload fixtures, since Selenium's file-input `send_keys` needs a real file on disk and pulling in a PDF library just for test fixtures felt like overkill), `sprint1/` (6 files: login, HR vacancies, HR CV upload, HR interviews, hiring manager flow, interviewer feedback), `sprint2/` (6 files: IT Admin users, audit logs, HR follow-ups/email, management reports, leadership reports, email history + comparison + branding), `requirements.txt`, `README.md`.

**Verification performed (no live app to test against):** every file parses (`ast.parse`), every file imports/executes at module scope with no `NameError`/`ImportError` under `importlib` (confirms every helper function referenced actually exists with the right name), and `helpers.make_test_pdf()` was smoke-tested standalone — writes a real, valid PDF (verified by parsing it back with `pypdf` and confirming the embedded text extracts correctly). One bug caught this way: an Esc-key test originally had a literal Unicode character where `Keys.ESCAPE` should have been (an authoring slip, not a selector-accuracy issue) — fixed and re-verified.

**Design choices flagged for the user:** several tests check a precondition (e.g. "a vacancy with no panel assigned yet", "a candidate who's already been emailed") and report a **skip with a stated reason** rather than a false failure when the current seed data doesn't happen to satisfy it — this keeps the suite meaningful regardless of exact DB state at run time, rather than being brittle against whatever's seeded. Each test opens/closes its own `webdriver.Chrome()` instance so state can't bleed between tests. The user still needs to `pip install -r requirements.txt` and have both `npm run dev` servers running on their own machine to actually execute these — flagged clearly in the README, along with guidance that a terminal screenshot of `N/M passed` plus 1-2 browser screenshots is stronger evidence for the report than a screenshot of the test source code alone.
